demand dates inside that window. Snapshot writes normalize blank payload run and allocation-unit fields to the requested scope and reject conflicting nonblank values, including a payload strategy that disagrees with the run. Writes additionally require the run to be active, while reads accept any legitimate lifecycle status so operators can inspect finalized results. Direct result writers cannot create orphaned snapshots, and result readers cannot silently consume orphaned, malformed, or cross-scope rows.

Run the checks with:

```text
npm test
npm run verify
```

`npm test` transpiles and runs the generated ABAP Unit harness plus the report/XML and SAP-integration repository contracts. `npm run verify` adds the lint pass. Open ABAP Core is pinned by commit as an npm development dependency; lint and transpilation use its installed local folder, so checks do not require a fresh GitHub clone after `npm install`. The transpiler emits the ABAP Unit runner into `output/`.

The `ZSTOCK_ALLOCATE` report prints the remaining and explicitly requested quantities plus the unit-scoped run totals, allocated quantity, shortage, last audit run ID, start/finish timestamps, requested-delivery window, and status/message after a successful allocation. Set `p_from` and `p_until` to allocate only open schedule lines inside an inclusive requested-date window; either bound may be blank. Reversed windows are rejected before allocation side effects. Completed runs include explicit success or shortage messages; rejected allocations are reported with the latest audited status/message, while the original service diagnostic is retained if the audit summary itself is unavailable. Set `p_shelf` to require that a selected batch expires at least that many days from the allocation date. Select `p_shg` with `p_shmax` to reject calculated shortages above an absolute quantity cap, or `p_covg` with `p_covmin` to reject allocations whose aggregate coverage is below the requested percentage, or `p_fullg` with `p_fmin` to require a minimum percentage of fully allocated demand lines, or `p_flg` with nonnegative `p_flmin` to require a minimum number of completely allocated demand lines, or `p_mflg` with nonnegative `p_mflmax` to cap completely allocated demand lines, or `p_dg` with `p_dmax` to cap the number of open demand lines, or `p_qg` with nonnegative `p_qmax` to cap aggregate requested quantity after demand units are converted to the allocation unit, or `p_ag` with nonnegative `p_amax` to cap calculated allocated quantity in that unit, or `p_lg` with nonnegative `p_lmax` to cap the number of positive-allocation demand lines, or `p_spg` with `p_spmax` from 0 through 100 percent to cap the aggregate shortage percentage, or `p_ug` with nonnegative `p_umax` to cap unallocated demand lines, or `p_pg` with nonnegative `p_pmax` to cap partial allocations, or `p_mg` with nonnegative `p_mmin` to require a minimum allocated quantity, or `p_ilg` with nonnegative `p_imin` to require a minimum number of fully or partially allocated demand lines, or `p_slg` with nonnegative `p_slmax` to cap shortage-bearing demand lines; all fifteen guards record a rejected audit row and stop before reservations or snapshot writes. Select `p_test` to preview and audit the calculation without reservation or snapshot dependencies, reservations, or `ZSTOCKALLOC` writes. Selecting `p_json` emits the completed allocation summary as one JSON object with `mode` set to `preview` or `execute`, scope, requested/remaining quantities, counters, run identity, timestamps, status, and diagnostic fields; authorization or execution failures emit a JSON error envelope.
Run audit rows now also persist full, partial, and unallocated demand-line counts plus both requested-delivery window bounds. Preview audit rows additionally persist a one-character preview marker and expose it through the audit run API, so consumers can distinguish simulated runs from operational runs even when both are finalized successfully. Rejection audits created by preview requests retain the same marker, including failures before a run can be started. `ZSTOCK_ALLOCATE` and `ZSTOCK_ALLOC_HISTORY` display those values alongside quantity metrics.

`ZSTOCK_ALLOC_WATCH` summaries now include the aggregate `demand_count` represented by the returned stale-run alerts and `deadline_count` for alerts with an effective requested deadline, plus `safety_stock_context` (`n/a`, one value, or `mixed`), adaptive priority/fair-share branch counts, `weighted_strategy_runs`, and weighted requested/allocated/shortage/coverage aggregates across the selected stale runs. Summary CSV uses schema `59`; JSON and NDJSON contexts use schema `62`, while each alert continues to expose its own demand-line count, persisted safety-stock policy, `weighted_strategy`, and `adaptive_branch`. Watch accepts optional requested-delivery horizon bounds `p_reqf`/`p_until`, `p_ovrd` overdue-horizon filtering, `p_dead` deadline-bearing filtering, and `p_prev` preview/operational run-type filtering (`P` or `O`), the zero-safe persisted safety-stock range `p_safon`/`p_saf`/`p_safto`, inclusive audit lifecycle start-date bounds `p_from`/`p_to`, inclusive audit lifecycle finish-date bounds `p_ffrom`/`p_fto`, `p_dcnt` demand-count-first ordering, `p_dage` deadline-age-first ordering, `p_due` earliest-requested-date ordering, and carries each alert's persisted `requested_on_from`/`requested_on_to` through human, CSV, JSON, typed JSON, and NDJSON output.

Current deadline-age contracts supersede the older schema references below: history accepts signed `p_dagef`/`p_daget` effective-deadline age bounds with optional `p_daged` reference date, `p_prev` preview/operational provenance filtering (`P` or `O`), adds `p_dcnt` demand-count-first and `p_dage` deadline-age-first ordering, and uses detail/summary schemas `27`/`44`; watch accepts the same `p_dagef`/`p_daget`/`p_daged` controls, adds `p_dcnt` demand-count-first and `p_dage` deadline-age-first ordering, and uses CSV/JSON/NDJSON schemas `57`/`60`. Both reports expose the selected age bounds and reference date in their filter provenance; positive ages are overdue days, negative ages are days remaining, and no-deadline rows are excluded when an age bound is active.

`ZSTOCK_ALLOC_RESULT` now accepts the same signed effective-deadline age controls for originating audit runs: `p_dagef`/`p_daget` are inclusive bounds and `p_daged` is an optional as-of date. `p_astat` filters persisted originating audit lifecycle status (`R`, `S`, `P`, or `E`) independently from snapshot-line `p_stat`; `p_prev` filters persisted preview (`P`) versus operational (`O`) audit provenance; `p_msg` applies a case-insensitive substring filter to the persisted audit message and `p_monly` keeps only runs with a nonblank message. The sink, exact-run reads, and `p_latest` apply these filters before snapshot pagination or newest-run selection. The sink applies the deadline predicate to persisted effective deadlines before row pagination; `p_dage` sorts result rows by originating-run signed deadline age, `p_due` sorts by the earliest effective originating-run deadline with no-deadline runs last, and `p_tdur` sorts by completed originating audit duration with unavailable durations last. Result detail/summary schemas are now `40`/`46`, with audit-status, preview provenance, message, age bounds, audit start/finish-date bounds, safety-stock policy and range, due-sort, duration-sort, reservation-age bounds, fair-share analytics, and reference-date provenance in human, CSV, JSON, NDJSON, and typed filter output.

Current result-report schema references supersede older paragraphs below: detail output is `38`, summary output is `44`, and `p_prev` filters persisted preview versus operational provenance through latest, exact-run, and snapshot-sink reads; `p_safon`/`p_saf`/`p_safto` filter persisted safety-stock policy through the same paths.

`ZSTOCK_ALLOC_PURGE` accepts the same signed effective-deadline age controls for preview and execution. It also accepts `p_rid` for a case-insensitive run-ID substring, `p_strat` for persisted allocation strategy (`P`, `F`, `N`, `S`, `L`, `B`, `E`, `A`, or `W`), `p_legacy` to retain only blank-strategy legacy runs, `p_msg` for a case-insensitive persisted diagnostic-message substring, and `p_monly` to retain only runs with a nonblank diagnostic. Optional `p_from`/`p_to` bound the persisted audit start date inclusively while `p_date` remains the exclusive cutoff; optional `p_ffrom`/`p_fto` bound the persisted finish date, excluding running rows when active; optional `p_tfrom`/`p_tto` bound completed audit duration in inclusive seconds; `p_max` caps the number of finalized runs selected for purge and `0` means unlimited. Reversed or empty start windows, reversed finish ranges, negative or reversed duration ranges, and negative maximum-run values are rejected. Runs without an effective deadline are excluded when an age bound is active; runs without a completed duration are excluded when a duration bound is active; runs with any nonblank snapshot `reservation_id` are protected from purge so the audit trail cannot be removed while a SAP reservation remains linked. Reversed bounds and an as-of date without a bound are rejected. Purge preview and execution JSON use schemas `27` and `28` respectively, in both typed and untyped modes, CSV uses schemas `25` and `26`, and human output carries the run-ID, strategy, legacy-strategy, message, start-date, finish-date, duration, age, maximum-run, reservation-protection, and capped-run provenance.

Audit runs also persist the reservation movement type and minimum shelf-life policy used by the allocation request. History detail and comparison output expose these policies so run reproduction and policy-change diagnosis do not depend on the original selection-screen values.

When `p_max` is positive, purge selects the oldest eligible finalized runs first by persisted start date, start time, and run ID; preview and execution use the same deterministic order. The cap limits only unprotected finalized selections, so later running, unknown, and reservation-protected rows remain visible in protection counts. Current purge output references supersede older detailed wording below: Preview JSON uses schema version `27`, and execution JSON uses schema version `28`; numeric `schema_version` `25` for preview or `26` for execution, and both modes expose `capped_audit_runs`.
Movement-type inputs are validated as numeric three-character SAP keys at the allocation service, direct goods-movement/reservation BAPI adapters, audit persistence, and allocation-snapshot persistence boundaries. Malformed service requests still create a durable error audit row, but the malformed value itself is not persisted.
The direct sales-order schedule-line adapter likewise rejects non-numeric or all-zero sales-document, item, and schedule-line keys before calling `BAPI_SALESORDER_CHANGE`.
The direct reservation, goods-movement, and sales-order adapters accept only canonical BAPI return statuses (`S`, `I`, `W`, `E`, `A`, `X`, or blank); unknown nonblank `RETURN-TYPE` values are treated as failures and rolled back rather than allowing a false-success commit. The allocation, goods-issue, and sales-order update reports normalize lowercase unit/order-type input before invoking SAP and use those canonical values in human-readable and machine-readable output.
Direct unit conversion, goods-issue, and reservation adapters canonicalize lowercase unit inputs to uppercase SAP unit keys before calling their standard APIs.
Audit history/summary unit filters and allocation-result detail filters are case-insensitive for allocation, order, and reservation units; stored SAP unit values remain unchanged in exports.
History, result, watch, purge, and comparison reports canonicalize their unit selection parameters before applying filters or rendering provenance, so lowercase user input cannot diverge from uppercase SAP keys. History and result also canonicalize lifecycle/status, sales-document-type, order-unit, and reservation-unit filters before querying or exporting them; comparison canonicalizes its change-type, old/new status, strategy, and sales-document-type filters as well.
Purge preview and execution apply the same case-insensitive unit normalization before selecting audit runs and linked snapshots.
The allocation service canonicalizes unit input at orchestration entry, so conversion, reservations, audit provenance, and persisted allocation snapshots use uppercase SAP unit keys consistently.
SAP stock and order-source adapters also canonicalize returned material and sales-order units before exposing them to allocation logic.
Direct audit lifecycle/rejection writes and allocation snapshot persistence apply the same normalization before database selection or writes.
Snapshot demand payloads are normalized locally before validation, so allocation, order, and reservation unit fields are stored with consistent uppercase SAP keys without mutating caller tables.
Snapshot comparison normalizes legacy unit casing before keying and diffing, preventing false changes caused only by SAP unit capitalization.
Rejected runs retain the same policy context and the original invalid requested-date bounds, allowing operators to diagnose failed input instead of losing the attempted request.

The `ZSTOCK_ALLOC_PURGE` report previews eligible audit-run and linked-snapshot counts when `p_exec` is not selected, including older running rows and runs with linked SAP reservations that remain protected, and executes audit retention only when `p_exec` is selected. It accepts optional exact `p_runid` and case-insensitive substring `p_rid` scopes plus policy filters `p_mvt` and nonnegative `p_shelf`, an optional finalized-run status filter `p_stat` (`S`, `P`, or `E`), `p_prev` for preview (`P`) or operational (`O`) runs, `p_strat` for one persisted allocation strategy (`P`, `F`, `N`, `S`, `L`, `B`, `E`, `A`, or `W`), `p_msg` for a case-insensitive persisted diagnostic-message substring, `p_monly` for nonblank diagnostics, `p_reqf`/`p_until` to match the persisted requested-delivery window, optional `p_from` to retain only runs on or after a lower audit start date, `p_ffrom`/`p_fto` to match an inclusive persisted finish-date range, `p_dead` to retain only runs with an effective requested deadline, and `p_ovrd`/`p_odate` to retain only deadlines before the selected as-of date. `p_from` must be before the exclusive cutoff `p_date`; `p_ffrom` must not be after `p_fto`; finish-date filtering excludes rows without a finish date; `p_odate` requires `p_ovrd`; omitted as-of dates use the SAP system date, and reversed requested-horizon bounds are rejected. The report rejects incomplete or future cutoff scopes, preserves unknown/corrupt lifecycle statuses for investigation, protects any run with a nonblank snapshot `reservation_id`, requires `S_TABU_NAM` delete authorization for both `ZSTOCKALLOC_RUN` and linked `ZSTOCKALLOC` rows, and reports execution failures with the returned authorization or persistence diagnostic. The audit API repeats the scope, exact/substring run-ID, policy, strategy, start-date, finish-date, requested-horizon, deadline-only, overdue/as-of, status, message, reservation-protection, and future-date safeguards for direct callers. Material, plant, storage location, optional batch/unit/exact-or-substring-run-ID/policy/status/preview/strategy/message/start-date/finish-date/requested-horizon/deadline-bearing/overdue filters, and the cutoff date scope the deletion; running and reservation-linked audit rows remain protected by the audit service.

Selecting `p_json` on `ZSTOCK_ALLOC_PURGE` emits a JSON preview or execution summary with the retention scope, `filters_applied`, a stable `filters` array for optional batch/unit/exact-or-substring-run-ID/policy/status/preview/max-runs/strategy/legacy-strategy/message/start-date/finish-date/duration/requested-horizon/deadline-bearing/overdue filters, explicit `run_id_filter`, `run_id_contains_filter`, `movement_type_filter`, `minimum_shelf_life_filter`, `status_filter`, `preview_filter`, `max_runs_filter`, `strategy_filter`, `legacy_strategy_filter`, `message_filter`, `message_only`, `requested_deadline_only`, `overdue_only`, `requested_overdue_as_of`, `requested_on_from_filter`, `requested_on_to_filter`, `start_date_from_filter`, `start_date_to_filter`, `finish_date_from_filter`, `finish_date_to_filter`, `audit_duration_from_filter`, and `audit_duration_to_filter` values, eligible/protected counts, preview-only `eligible_success_runs`, `eligible_partial_runs`, `eligible_error_runs`, `protected_unknown_runs`, and `protected_reservation_runs`, execution-only `deleted_success_runs`, `deleted_partial_runs`, `deleted_error_runs`, `protected_running_runs`, `protected_unknown_runs`, and `protected_reservation_runs`, linked snapshots, deleted audit runs, deleted result snapshots, and `capped_audit_runs`; validation, authorization, preview, and execution failures emit a JSON error envelope. `p_max` caps the selected finalized runs in both preview and execution; `0` leaves the retention scope unlimited. Typed output additionally exposes string exact/substring run-ID, movement/status/preview/strategy/message/start-date/finish-date/horizon values, boolean legacy-strategy/message/deadline/overdue filters plus numeric/null `max_runs`, the selected as-of date, and numeric/null `minimum_shelf_life`, `audit_duration_from`, and `audit_duration_to` in `filter_values`. Preview JSON uses schema version `27`, and execution JSON uses schema version `28` for filter provenance, run-ID/policy/status/preview/max-runs/strategy/legacy-strategy/message/start-date/finish-date/duration/horizon fields, typed filter values, per-status deleted counts, protected-row counts, and the additional `deleted_result_snapshots` number.

Selecting `p_csv` on `ZSTOCK_ALLOC_PURGE` emits a one-row semicolon-delimited preview or execution summary with a stable header, including generated date/time fields, `filters_applied`, a pipe-delimited `filters` field, explicit `run_id_filter`, `run_id_contains_filter`, `movement_type_filter`, `minimum_shelf_life_filter`, `status_filter`, `preview_filter`, `max_runs_filter`, `strategy_filter`, `legacy_strategy_filter`, `message_filter`, `message_only`, `requested_deadline_only`, `overdue_only`, `requested_overdue_as_of`, `requested_on_from_filter`, `requested_on_to_filter`, `start_date_from_filter`, `start_date_to_filter`, `finish_date_from_filter`, `finish_date_to_filter`, `audit_duration_from_filter`, and `audit_duration_to_filter` values, and numeric `schema_version` `25` for preview or `26` for execution. Preview adds eligible success/partial/error and protected unknown-status/reservation counts; execution adds deleted success/partial/error counts, protected running/unknown-status/reservation counts, `capped_audit_runs`, and `deleted_result_snapshots`; numeric counts use a locale-independent period decimal separator and non-applicable columns are `n/a`. CSV and JSON output are mutually exclusive.

When purge CSV processing fails before a summary can be produced, it emits the stable error envelope `mode;status;schema_version;message` with `status` set to `error` and schema `25` for preview or `26` for execution instead of human-readable prose.

Selecting `p_typed` with purge `p_json` emits retention counts as JSON numbers and adds a grouped `filter_values` object with string exact/substring run-ID/movement/status/preview/strategy/message/start-date/finish-date/horizon values, boolean `legacy_strategy`/`message_only`/`requested_deadline_only`/`overdue_only` plus the string `preview_filter`, the requested overdue as-of date, and numeric/null shelf-life and duration bounds while preserving the existing string-valued JSON contract by default. It requires `p_json` and cannot be combined with CSV output.

All purge JSON success responses include generated date/time markers, numeric `schema_version: 27` for preview or `28` for execution, `filters_applied`, a stable `filters` array, and explicit exact/substring run-ID/policy/status/preview/max-runs/strategy/legacy-strategy/start-date/finish-date/duration/requested-horizon/deadline-bearing/overdue filter values; typed responses additionally include boolean `typed: true`, `filter_values`, and numeric reservation-protection and capped-run counts.

Purge JSON errors use schema `25` in preview mode and `26` in execution mode, including validation, authorization, preview, and execution failures.

Selecting `p_csv` on `ZSTOCK_ALLOCATE` emits the completed preview or execution summary as one semicolon-delimited row with generated date/time, numeric `schema_version` `49`, scope, normalized strategy, movement type, minimum shelf-life policy, the entered `safety_stock` floor, shortage-guard state and maximum, coverage-guard state and minimum percentage, full-line guard state and minimum percentage, minimum and maximum full-line-count guard state and thresholds, demand guard state and maximum count, requested-quantity guard state and maximum, allocated-quantity guard state and maximum, minimum-allocation guard state and minimum, minimum-allocation-line guard state and minimum, allocated-line guard state and maximum, shortage-percentage guard state and maximum, unallocated-line guard state and maximum, partial-line guard state and maximum, shortage-line guard state and maximum, and the entered requested-date filter, remaining/requested quantities, total/status/per-strategy run counters, `completion_pct`, `success_rate_pct`, `partial_rate_pct`, `error_rate_pct`, per-strategy requested/allocated/shortage/coverage totals, aggregate requested/allocated/shortage/coverage totals, `shortage_pct`, full/partial/unallocated line counts, aggregate `demand_count`, `deadline_count`, current/latest run IDs, latest-run strategy, lifecycle timestamps, `last_requested_deadline`, `earliest_requested_deadline`, `latest_requested_deadline`, signed `last_deadline_age_days`, `oldest_deadline_age_days`, and `newest_deadline_age_days`, `deadline_age_reference_date`, `last_duration_seconds`, `average_duration_seconds`, `minimum_duration_seconds`, `maximum_duration_seconds`, `completed_duration_runs`, `oldest_running_age_seconds`, `oldest_running_run_id`, `newest_running_age_seconds`, `newest_running_run_id`, status, and diagnostic message. Numeric fields use the locale-independent CSV number contract, and CSV is mutually exclusive with `p_json`.

When allocation CSV processing fails before a summary can be produced, it emits the same `mode;status;schema_version;message` error envelope with `status` set to `error` and schema `49`.

Allocation JSON errors use schema `49` and preserve `run_id` when an allocation run was created before the failure.

Selecting `p_typed` with allocation `p_json` emits requested, remaining, allocation quantities, counters, `completion_pct`, `success_rate_pct`, `partial_rate_pct`, `error_rate_pct`, aggregate coverage and `shortage_pct`, per-strategy quantity/coverage totals, full/partial/unallocated line counts, aggregate `demand_count`, `deadline_count`, `minimum_shelf_life_days`, `safety_stock`, `shortage_guard_active`, `maximum_shortage`, `coverage_guard_active`, `minimum_coverage_pct`, `full_line_guard_active`, `minimum_full_line_pct`, `full_line_count_guard_active`, `minimum_full_lines`, `maximum_full_line_count_guard_active`, `maximum_full_lines`, `demand_guard_active`, `maximum_demand_count`, `quantity_guard_active`, `maximum_requested_quantity`, `allocation_guard_active`, `maximum_allocated_quantity`, `minimum_allocation_guard_active`, `minimum_allocated_quantity`, `minimum_allocation_line_guard_active`, `minimum_allocated_lines`, `allocation_line_guard_active`, `maximum_allocated_lines`, `shortage_pct_guard_active`, `maximum_shortage_pct`, `unallocated_line_guard_active`, `maximum_unallocated_lines`, `partial_line_guard_active`, `maximum_partial_lines`, `shortage_line_guard_active`, `maximum_shortage_lines`, `last_requested_deadline`, `earliest_requested_deadline`, `latest_requested_deadline`, signed numeric-or-null `last_deadline_age_days`, `oldest_deadline_age_days`, and `newest_deadline_age_days`, plus `deadline_age_reference_date`, `last_duration_seconds`, `average_duration_seconds`, `minimum_duration_seconds`, `maximum_duration_seconds`, `completed_duration_runs`, `oldest_running_age_seconds`, `oldest_running_run_id`, `newest_running_age_seconds`, and `newest_running_run_id` as JSON values while preserving the existing string-valued JSON contract by default. Allocation JSON uses schema version `49`; typed output additionally includes boolean `typed: true` and numeric fields, and it requires `p_json` and cannot be combined with CSV output.

All allocation JSON success responses include generated date/time markers, numeric `schema_version: 49`, movement type, minimum shelf-life policy, safety-stock floor, shortage-guard state and maximum, coverage-guard state and minimum percentage, full-line guard state and minimum percentage, minimum and maximum full-line-count guard state and thresholds, demand guard state and maximum count, requested-quantity guard state and maximum, allocated-quantity guard state and maximum, minimum-allocation guard state and minimum, minimum-allocation-line guard state and minimum, allocated-line guard state and maximum, shortage-percentage guard state and maximum, unallocated-line guard state and maximum, partial-line guard state and maximum, shortage-line guard state and maximum, and the entered requested-date filter; typed responses additionally include boolean `typed: true`. Both JSON shapes expose `completion_pct`, `success_rate_pct`, `partial_rate_pct`, `error_rate_pct`, aggregate `coverage_pct`, `shortage_pct`, full/partial/unallocated line counts, aggregate `demand_count`, `deadline_count`, `last_requested_deadline`, `earliest_requested_deadline`, `latest_requested_deadline`, signed `last_deadline_age_days`, `oldest_deadline_age_days`, and `newest_deadline_age_days`, `deadline_age_reference_date`, `last_duration_seconds`, `average_duration_seconds`, `minimum_duration_seconds`, `maximum_duration_seconds`, `completed_duration_runs`, `oldest_running_age_seconds`, `oldest_running_run_id`, `newest_running_age_seconds`, `newest_running_run_id`, and the per-strategy coverage fields.

The allocation, history, and result reports require `S_TABU_NAM` read authorization for the audit or result table they query and report missing authorization cleanly. The SAP stock source now checks activity `03` for `MARA` and `MARC` for every read, `MARD` for non-batch reads, and `MCHB` plus `MCHA` for batch reads, while the SAP order source checks activity `03` for `VBAK`, `VBAP`, and `VBEP`, and the unit-conversion adapter checks `MARA` and `MARM`, through injectable source-read authority ports. Allocation execution additionally requires activity `02` write authorization for `ZSTOCKALLOC_RUN` audit rows, activity `02` change authorization for `ZSTOCKALLOC` result rows, and activity `06` delete authorization for snapshot replacement; purge enforces activity `06` retention authorization for both affected tables at the API boundary. The concrete SAP adapters also default to their standard authorization ports when called directly without dependency injection, so callers cannot bypass these checks by omitting an optional port. Allocation execution commits the running audit row before reservation or snapshot side effects and rolls back failed snapshot persistence before recording the final failure, with rollback failures retained in the diagnostic message.

The read-only `ZSTOCK_ALLOC_HISTORY` report lists the scoped run ID, status, unit, persisted strategy, requested-delivery window, available stock, demand count, deadline-bearing run count, allocated quantity, shortage, start and finish timestamps, elapsed duration in seconds, and diagnostic message for operational review; running rows have no finish timestamp or duration. It prints lifecycle, quantity, allocation coverage percentage, full/partial/unallocated demand totals, and per-strategy run counts for the filtered population before the detail rows, omitting quantity aggregation when the rows use different units. `p_runid` optionally selects one exact audit run, while `p_reqf` and `p_until` filter the output to one persisted requested-delivery window; reversed horizon bounds are rejected. `p_stat` accepts `R`, `S`, `P`, or `E`, and `p_strat` accepts `P`, `F`, `N`, `S`, `L`, `B`, `E`, `A`, or `W`; `p_from` and `p_to` optionally filter the output by run-start date, `p_ffrom`/`p_fto` optionally filter by finish date, `p_shf`/`p_sht` optionally filter by inclusive shortage range, `p_af`/`p_at` optionally filter by inclusive allocated-quantity range, `p_avf`/`p_avt` optionally filter by inclusive available-stock range, `p_qf`/`p_qt` optionally filter by inclusive derived requested-quantity range (`allocated + shortage`), `p_dfrom`/`p_dto` optionally filter by inclusive demand-count range, `p_covf`/`p_covt` optionally filter by inclusive allocation-coverage percentage from 0 to 100, `p_spf`/`p_spt` optionally filter by inclusive shortage percentage from 0 to 100, and `p_dagef`/`p_daget` optionally filter by signed effective-deadline age relative to `p_daged` (or the SAP system date): positive values are overdue days and negative values are days remaining. Runs without an effective deadline are excluded by a deadline-age bound, and reversed age bounds or an as-of date without an age bound are rejected. Runs with zero requested quantity are excluded when a coverage or shortage-percentage bound is supplied because the ratio is not applicable. Selecting `p_shrt` orders history by descending shortage, then start chronology and run ID; without it, the default newest-started-first order is retained. Requesting a finish window excludes still-running rows. History read failures are reported cleanly. Direct summaries without a unit remain safe across parallel units: `mixed_units` is set and aggregate requested/allocated/shortage quantities are blank when runs use different units. The audit summary API exposes `requested` explicitly as allocated plus shortage for unit-consistent populations.

`p_tfrom`/`p_tto` optionally filter completed history rows by inclusive elapsed duration in seconds; running rows are excluded whenever a duration bound is requested, and negative or reversed bounds are rejected.

Selecting `p_tdur` orders history by elapsed duration descending, with start timestamp and run ID as deterministic tie-breakers. Coverage-first ordering takes precedence, followed by shortage-percentage, demand-count, deadline-age, requested-date, status, duration, and shortage-first ordering.

Selecting history `p_sstat` orders runs by diagnostic risk: errors, partial runs, running runs, then successes; shortage and newest-start tie-breakers remain deterministic. Coverage-first ordering takes precedence, followed by shortage-percentage, demand-count, deadline-age, requested-date, status, duration, and shortage-first ordering.

Selecting history `p_dcnt` orders runs by descending demand count, then shortage and newest-start chronology; it is reported as `demand_count` in the sort metadata and takes precedence over deadline age, requested date, status, duration, and shortage.

`p_stale` optionally keeps only running audit rows whose elapsed age is at least the supplied number of seconds; `p_age_to` optionally keeps only running rows no older than the supplied number of seconds. Negative thresholds are rejected, either bound can be used independently, and a supplied upper bound cannot be below the stale lower bound. These filters are independent of finalized-run duration filtering. Audit finalization rejects partial (`P`) runs with zero shortage so lifecycle status and metrics remain consistent.

`ZSTOCK_ALLOC_WATCH` is a focused operational view for stale active runs. It accepts the same material/plant/storage-location scope, optional batch and unit, optional exact `p_runid`, an optional case-insensitive run-ID substring `p_runq`, and an optional case-insensitive diagnostic-message substring `p_msg`, `p_monly` to retain only runs with a diagnostic message, `p_dead` to retain only runs with an effective requested deadline, `p_safon` with nonnegative inclusive `p_saf`/`p_safto` bounds to filter persisted safety-stock policy (the switch is required even for an exact zero range), an optional `p_strat` filter (`P`, `F`, `N`, `S`, `L`, `B`, `E`, `A`, or `W`), `p_prev` preview/operational run-type filter (`P` or `O`), or `p_legacy` to retain only blank-strategy legacy runs, optional inclusive persisted audit lifecycle start-date bounds `p_from`/`p_to`, optional inclusive persisted audit lifecycle finish-date bounds `p_ffrom`/`p_fto`, optional nonnegative `p_shf`/`p_sht` minimum/maximum-shortage filters, optional nonnegative `p_dfrom`/`p_dto` minimum/maximum demand-count filters, optional nonnegative `p_avf`/`p_avt` minimum/maximum available-stock filters, optional nonnegative `p_qf`/`p_qt` minimum/maximum requested-quantity filters, optional nonnegative `p_af`/`p_at` minimum/maximum allocated-quantity filters, optional `p_covf` and `p_covt` minimum/maximum coverage filters from 0 to 100, a nonnegative `p_stale` threshold (default 3600 seconds), an optional nonnegative `p_age_to` maximum running-age bound, an optional nonnegative `p_skip` offset, an optional `p_max` alert limit, `p_shrt` to sort by shortage, `p_cov` to sort by lowest applicable coverage first, `p_spct` to sort by highest applicable shortage percentage first, `p_dcnt` to sort by highest demand count first, `p_dage` to sort by most overdue effective deadline first, and `p_new` to sort newest-started stale runs first. When multiple sort checkboxes are selected, coverage sorting takes precedence over shortage percentage, which takes precedence over demand count, deadline age, shortage, and newest-first; each mode retains deterministic age/time/run-ID ties. The `p_legacy` filter cannot be combined with `p_strat`; all range bounds reject reversed ranges; shortage-percentage bounds are restricted to 0 through 100; when `p_age_to` is supplied, it must not be below `p_stale`. Alerts are calculated through the canonical running-age API; the default is oldest-first. Human, CSV, and JSON output are supported; current JSON and NDJSON contexts use schema version `62`, include explicit `material`, `plant`, `storage_location`, `batch`, and `requested_unit` scope fields plus `filters_applied` and a stable `filters` array alongside `sort_mode`, filter provenance including `preview_filter`, `message_filter`, `run_id_contains_filter`, `legacy_strategy_filter`, `start_date_from_filter`, `start_date_to_filter`, `finish_date_from_filter`, and `finish_date_to_filter`, `message_only`, `minimum_demand_count`, `maximum_demand_count`, `minimum_available_stock`, `maximum_available_stock`, `minimum_requested_quantity`, `maximum_requested_quantity`, `minimum_allocated_quantity`, `maximum_allocated_quantity`, `minimum_shortage`, `maximum_shortage`, `minimum_shortage_pct`, `maximum_shortage_pct`, `minimum_coverage`, `maximum_coverage`, `safety_stock_filter`, `minimum_safety_stock_filter`, and `maximum_safety_stock_filter`, `offset`, `has_more`, `next_offset`, `has_previous`, `previous_offset`, `page_number`, `page_count`, `last_offset`, pre-limit `candidate_count`, a boolean `limited` truncation marker, returned `alert_count`, aggregate `demand_count`, `deadline_count`, `safety_stock_context`, page-scoped earliest/latest effective requested deadlines, and unit-safe quantity/coverage/shortage-percentage aggregates with `unit` and `mixed_units`, and oldest/newest alert age (`null` when unavailable). Returned detail alerts include a one-based stable global `rank` after sorting, persisted `movement_type`, `min_shelf_life`, and `safety_stock`, and typed `coverage_pct`, `shortage_pct`, `min_shelf_life`, and `safety_stock` (`null` for zero-request runs only applies to the percentage fields). Selecting `p_typed` with `p_json` emits numeric filter bounds and JSON `null` for blank numeric filters while preserving string filters by default; JSON and NDJSON expose `next_offset`, `has_previous`, `previous_offset`, `page_number`, `page_count`, and `last_offset` navigation fields; typed JSON and NDJSON also expose a grouped numeric `filter_values` object containing the numeric bounds and pagination values; it requires JSON mode. Selecting `p_ndjson` with `p_json` emits one complete self-describing alert object per line, repeats the filter, page, aggregate, and deadline-range context on each line, emits no array delimiters, and is mutually exclusive with `p_sum`; an empty feed emits no data lines. Detail and summary CSV use schema version `59`; watch JSON and NDJSON contexts use schema version `62`. Blank filters are reported as `n/a`; the legacy-strategy and requested-deadline-only filters are reported as booleans; exact and substring run-ID filters can be combined. Zero-request runs are excluded when a coverage or shortage-percentage bound is supplied because the corresponding ratio is not applicable.

The current watch sort contract additionally supports `p_dcnt` demand-count-first ordering; current export schemas are CSV `59` and JSON/NDJSON `62`, with persisted safety-stock policy, safety-stock range, audit start/finish-date bounds, adaptive branch provenance, and weighted-strategy telemetry included in alert and summary context.

`p_sht` adds an optional maximum shortage bound to the watch report; it is validated with `p_shf` and exported as `maximum_shortage`.

`p_spf`/`p_spt` add inclusive shortage-percentage bounds from 0 to 100; zero-request runs are excluded because the percentage is not applicable, reversed bounds are rejected, and `minimum_shortage_pct`/`maximum_shortage_pct` provenance fields are exported.

`p_spct` orders watch alerts by descending applicable shortage percentage, then shortage, age, start chronology, and run ID; `p_dcnt` orders by descending demand count; `p_dage` orders by signed deadline age with the most overdue deadlines first and rows without deadlines last. Non-applicable zero-request alerts sort last. `p_spct` takes precedence below coverage sorting and above demand-count, deadline-age, and absolute-shortage sorting; demand-count sorting takes precedence above deadline-age and absolute-shortage sorting. Detail outputs expose typed `shortage_pct` (`null`/`n/a` when unavailable), persisted movement type, minimum shelf-life and safety-stock policies, requested-delivery horizon, its effective `requested_deadline`, `deadline_age_reference_date`, the boolean `deadline_age_mixed`, and `weighted_strategy`; summary outputs expose aggregate `shortage_pct`, `deadline_count`, adaptive priority/fair-share branch counts, `weighted_strategy_runs`, weighted requested/allocated/shortage/coverage aggregates, the earliest/latest effective requested deadlines, oldest/newest signed deadline ages, safety-stock context, the same reference date represented by the page, and `deadline_age_mixed`. Current watch contracts use CSV schema version `59` and JSON/NDJSON schema version `62`.

The current watch schema supersedes older references in this document: detail and summary CSV use schema `59`, while JSON and NDJSON use schema `62`. The `p_dcnt` sort is deterministic and orders demand count descending, then shortage, running age, start timestamp, and run ID. The `p_dage` sort is deterministic and orders effective deadline ages descending (most overdue first), then deadline date, shortage, running age, start timestamp, and run ID; alerts without an effective deadline remain last.

Watch JSON errors use schema `61`, matching its JSON and NDJSON contexts.

Watch also accepts exact persisted movement-type `p_mvt` and nonnegative minimum-shelf-life `p_shelf` policy filters. Their values are passed through the audit read API and exposed as `movement_type_filter` and `minimum_shelf_life_filter` in every watch export mode.
Movement-type filters are validated as numeric three-character SAP keys across audit history, purge, watch, and allocation-result reads; malformed `p_mvt`/`p_rmov` values return explicit validation diagnostics instead of silently producing no rows.

`p_legacy` isolates active runs with a blank persisted strategy for pre-strategy history; it cannot be combined with `p_strat` and is exported as `legacy_strategy_filter`.

`p_msg` optionally filters history by a case-insensitive substring of the persisted diagnostic message, which is useful for isolating failure and cleanup reasons.

Selecting `p_monly` keeps only runs with a nonblank persisted diagnostic message; it can be combined with `p_msg` to narrow that diagnostic population further.

`p_rid` optionally keeps history rows whose run ID contains the supplied fragment, case-insensitively. The direct audit read port applies the same filter, so report and API consumers see identical results.
Direct sales-order schedule-line mutations can inject `V_VBAK_AAT` authorization for activity `02` using the supplied sales document type.

The `ZSTOCK_ALLOC_ORDER_UPDATE` report changes one sales-order schedule-line quantity through the authorized adapter only when `p_exec` is selected; without the checkbox it performs no mutation and reports BAPI failures cleanly.

Selecting `p_json` on `ZSTOCK_ALLOC_ORDER_UPDATE` emits a structured success object or JSON error envelope.

Selecting `p_csv` on `ZSTOCK_ALLOC_ORDER_UPDATE` emits one stable semicolon-delimited row with generated date/time, numeric `schema_version` `1`, the requested schedule-line change, quantity, status (`not_executed`, `success`, or `error`), and diagnostic message. CSV and JSON output are mutually exclusive.

Selecting `p_typed` with order-update `p_json` emits `quantity` as a JSON number while preserving the default string encoding. It requires `p_json` and cannot be combined with CSV output.

All order-update JSON success responses include generated date/time markers and numeric `schema_version: 1`; typed responses additionally include boolean `typed: true`.

Invalid typed/CSV combinations use the shared `mode;status;schema_version;message` CSV error envelope.

The `ZSTOCK_ALLOC_GOODS_ISSUE` report posts one authorized goods issue through `BAPI_GOODSMVT_CREATE` only when `p_exec` is selected; without the checkbox it performs no mutation and reports BAPI failures cleanly. Successful output includes both the material-document number and document year from `GOODSMVT_HEADRET`.

Selecting `p_json` on `ZSTOCK_ALLOC_GOODS_ISSUE` emits a structured success object or JSON error envelope, including the complete material-document identity.

Selecting `p_csv` on `ZSTOCK_ALLOC_GOODS_ISSUE` emits one stable semicolon-delimited row with generated date/time, numeric `schema_version` `1`, stock scope, quantity, material-document identity, status (`not_executed`, `success`, or `error`), and diagnostic message. CSV and JSON output are mutually exclusive.

Selecting `p_typed` with goods-issue `p_json` emits `quantity` and `document_year` as JSON numbers while preserving the default string encoding. It requires `p_json` and cannot be combined with CSV output.

All goods-issue JSON success responses include generated date/time markers and numeric `schema_version: 1`; typed responses additionally include boolean `typed: true`.

Invalid typed/CSV combinations use the shared `mode;status;schema_version;message` CSV error envelope.

The read-only `ZSTOCK_ALLOC_RESULT` report displays the scoped per-demand allocation status, requested delivery date, normalized allocation unit, original sales-order unit, requested/allocated/shortage quantities, sales-document type, reservation ID and lifecycle metadata (date, movement type, and unit), and audit run ID from `ZSTOCKALLOC`.

The read-only `ZSTOCK_ALLOC_COMPARE` report compares two persisted allocation runs for a common or independent old/new plant, material, and storage-location scope. `p_old` and `p_new` identify the runs; `p_meins` optionally narrows the comparison to one allocation unit, and `p_charg` retains batch scope. By default it emits only changed demand keys: `A` means added in the new run, `R` means removed, and `C` means the key exists in both runs but its persisted source identity, allocation strategy, requested date, priority, status, quantities, reservation ID, or reservation metadata changed. Every detail row also carries deterministic pipe-delimited `change_reasons` (`added`, `removed`, or the changed field names). `p_reason` filters to one validated reason such as `reservation_id`, `allocated`, `sales_document`, or `allocation_strategy`; `p_all` includes unchanged keys as `U` with an empty reason list. CSV and JSON detail rows expose old/new source-document, schedule-line, order-unit, and reservation metadata alongside the existing quantity/delta fields, while `p_typed` emits numeric priorities and quantities. `p_chg` restricts output to one change type, while `p_skip` and `p_max` provide offset/limit pagination; `p_meta` wraps JSON rows with `total_rows`, `offset`, `max_rows`, `old_run`, `new_run`, `change_type`, `reason_filter`, `include_unchanged`, authorized old/new lifecycle status, strategy, timestamps, requested-delivery horizons, available-stock baselines, and messages, plus `old_reconciliation`/`new_reconciliation` and snapshot-row counts so consumers can distinguish the filtered total from the returned page and verify each snapshot against its audit counters and quantities. `p_guard` makes that check strict and returns an error instead of comparison output when either run is inconsistent. CSV and human output expose the same old/new run and reconciliation context; plain JSON arrays and NDJSON retain their existing row-only shape. `p_sum` emits aggregate counts, old/new/delta requested, allocated, and shortage quantities, plus old/new coverage and shortage percentages and new-minus-old percentage deltas; its totals are calculated before pagination, and it works in human, CSV, and JSON modes (with `p_meta`, JSON nests the summary beside the comparison page context). When a comparison spans multiple allocation units, summary quantity totals and percentages are suppressed as non-comparable, with `mixed_units` exposed and `n/a`/`null` output as appropriate. `p_ndjson` streams one complete comparison row object per line for large consumers, preserves regular or typed row encoding, requires JSON, and cannot be combined with `p_meta` or `p_sum`. Comparison reads use the existing result-read authorization boundary and reject duplicate snapshot keys or identical old/new run IDs.

`ZSTOCK_ALLOC_RESULT` accepts an optional `p_meins` unit filter plus optional `p_runid`, `p_stat`, `p_vbeln`, `p_auart`, `p_posnr`, `p_etenr`, `p_ounit`, `p_order`, `p_resid`, `p_rmov`, `p_runit`, `p_rsv`, `p_rfrom`, `p_rto`, `p_from`, `p_to`, `p_priof`, `p_priot`, `p_shf`, `p_sht`, `p_qf`, `p_qt`, `p_af`, `p_at`, `p_spf`, and `p_spt` filters plus `p_pri`, `p_sstat`, `p_date`, `p_rdate`, `p_shrt`, `p_cov`, `p_spct`, `p_big`, and `p_done` sort checkboxes. When `p_meins` is blank, parallel result snapshots in all allocation units are displayed together. `p_runid` restricts output to one persisted audit run and prints that ¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬Ãƒâ€¦Ã‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬Ãƒâ€¦Ã‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â¦ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬Ãƒâ€¦Ã‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¦ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬Ãƒâ€¦Ã‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¾Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¾Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â¦Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¦ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¾Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬Ãƒâ€¦Ã‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â¦ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â¦Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬Ãƒâ€¦Ã‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢s lifecycle context, audit counters, and diagnostic message, even when the run has no snapshot rows yet. When `p_runid` is the only detail filter, the report also reconciles snapshot line/outcome counts and allocated/shortage quantities against the audit row and prints `OK` or `MISMATCH`; filtered views intentionally omit that comparison. `p_stat` accepts `F`, `P`, and `U` to show only fully allocated, partially allocated, or unallocated result lines, `p_vbeln` selects the persisted sales document and must be a nonzero ten-character identity, `p_auart` selects its document type, `p_posnr` selects its item, `p_etenr` selects its schedule line, `p_ounit` selects the original sales-order unit, `p_order` selects one derived demand key, `p_resid` selects a SAP reservation document and rejects malformed numeric or all-zero identities, `p_rmov` selects a reservation movement type, `p_runit` selects the persisted reservation unit, `p_rsv` keeps only rows with a reservation ID, `p_rfrom`/`p_rto` select an inclusive reservation-posting-date window, `p_from`/`p_to` select an inclusive requested-delivery-date window, `p_priof`/`p_priot` select an inclusive allocator-priority range, `p_shf`/`p_sht` select an inclusive shortage-quantity range, `p_qf`/`p_qt` select an inclusive requested-quantity range, `p_af`/`p_at` select an inclusive allocated-quantity range, and `p_spf`/`p_spt` select an inclusive shortage-percentage range from 0 to 100. Selecting `p_pri` orders rows by allocation unit, persisted priority, and order key; selecting `p_sstat` orders unallocated rows first, then partial and full rows, followed by shortage/date/unit/priority/order-key tie-breakers; selecting `p_cov` orders by ascending coverage; selecting `p_spct` orders by descending shortage percentage, then shortage, requested date, allocation unit, priority, and order key; selecting `p_big` orders by descending requested quantity; selecting `p_done` orders by descending allocated quantity; selecting `p_date` orders globally by requested delivery date; selecting `p_rdate` orders by reservation date; and selecting `p_shrt` orders by descending shortage. Sort precedence is `p_pri`, `p_sstat`, `p_cov`, `p_spct`, `p_big`, `p_done`, `p_date`, `p_rdate`, then `p_shrt`. The report prints unit-safe requested, allocated, shortage, and allocation coverage totals plus the exact order ID and persisted allocation priority for reconciliation.

The current result selection surface also includes `p_astat` for persisted audit lifecycle status (`R`, `S`, `P`, or `E`), independently from snapshot-line `p_stat` (`F`, `P`, or `U`). `p_latest` applies all selected originating-run policy filters before newest-run selection, and every result output mode displays or carries the audit-status filter provenance alongside the existing row and audit context.

Result sorting also supports `p_dcnt` (highest originating audit demand count first), `p_dage` (most overdue originating audit deadline first), `p_due` (earliest effective originating audit deadline first), and `p_tdur` (slowest completed originating audit run first; running or unavailable durations remain last). Their precedence is coverage, shortage percentage, demand count, deadline age, requested deadline, audit duration, requested quantity, allocated quantity, requested date, reservation date, then shortage.

Comparison reconciliation now also checks the persisted audit run's requested total and validates each snapshot row's unit and quantity/status relationship. Contextual comparison exports expose `old_snapshot_requested` and `new_snapshot_requested` beside the snapshot row counts, so unit drift, requested-quantity drift, and malformed snapshot lines are distinguishable from allocated or shortage drift.

`p_shrt` sorts comparison rows by highest applicable shortage first. Added and changed rows use the new snapshot shortage; removed rows use the old shortage. Equal shortages use requested date, change type, allocation unit, and order ID tie-breakers, and pagination is applied after this sort. This introduced the comparison `sort_mode` contract, now at schema `56` after the due-date, coverage, shortage-worsening, coverage-deterioration, shortage-percentage-deterioration, status-regression, requested-quantity-delta, allocation-status-filter, originating-audit-status-filter, derived-reason, relative-shortage, row-metric, percentage-delta, and summary-metric extensions below.

`p_wors` sorts comparison rows by the largest shortage increase (`new_shortage - old_shortage`) first. Added and changed rows use new shortage values, removals use their old shortage as the last-known value, and improvements or unchanged rows naturally fall after worsening rows. Current shortage, requested date, change type, allocation unit, and order ID provide deterministic ties; pagination is applied after sorting.

`p_cw` sorts comparison rows by the largest allocation-coverage deterioration (`old_coverage_pct - new_coverage_pct`) first. Both snapshot sides must have positive requested quantities for a deterioration value; added, removed, and otherwise non-comparable rows sort after applicable changes. Lower current coverage, shortage, requested date, change type, allocation unit, and order ID provide deterministic ties. `p_wors` takes precedence over `p_cw`, which takes precedence over `p_spw`, `p_qd`, `p_cov`, `p_spct`, `p_due`, and `p_shrt`.

`p_spw` sorts comparison rows by the largest shortage-percentage deterioration (`new_shortage_pct - old_shortage_pct`) first. Both snapshot sides must have positive requested quantities; non-comparable added, removed, or zero-request rows sort last. Current shortage percentage, absolute shortage, requested date, change type, allocation unit, and order ID provide deterministic ties. Sort precedence is `p_wors`, `p_cw`, `p_spw`, `p_sreg`, `p_qd`, `p_cov`, `p_spct`, `p_due`, then `p_shrt`.

`p_sreg` sorts comparable rows by allocation-status regression: full (`F`) to unallocated (`U`) ranks ahead of full to partial, stable outcomes, and improvements. Added and removed rows without both statuses sort last; current status severity, shortage, requested date, change type, allocation unit, and order ID provide deterministic ties. Pagination is applied after the regression sort.

`p_qd` sorts comparison rows by descending requested-quantity delta (`new_requested - old_requested`). Added rows therefore sort by their positive new demand, removed rows by their negative old demand, and unchanged rows at zero; current requested quantity, shortage, requested date, change type, allocation unit, and order ID provide deterministic ties. Sort precedence is `p_wors`, `p_cw`, `p_spw`, `p_qd`, `p_cov`, `p_spct`, `p_due`, then `p_shrt`.

Comparison accepts `p_ost` and `p_nst` to retain only rows with the selected old or new allocation outcome (`F` full, `P` partial, or `U` unallocated). It also accepts `p_oast` and `p_nast` to require the old and new originating audit runs to have lifecycle status `R`, `S`, `P`, or `E`, independently. All four filters are case-insensitive; snapshot filters apply after change/reason classification, while audit-status filters constrain run and snapshot resolution before comparison. The selected values appear as explicit old/new allocation and audit-status filter fields in human, CSV, JSON, typed, and NDJSON output.

Comparison `change_reasons` now include `coverage` when the zero-safe allocated/requested coverage ratio changes or becomes applicable/unavailable, and `shortage_pct` when the corresponding shortage/requested ratio changes; `p_reason=coverage` and `p_reason=shortage_pct` filter to those rows.

`p_due` sorts comparison rows by the earliest available requested date, using the new snapshot for added/changed rows and the old snapshot for removals. Rows without a requested date sort last; shortage, change type, allocation unit, and order ID provide deterministic ties. `p_due` takes precedence over `p_shrt` when both are selected; it introduced schema `42` before the current coverage extension.

`p_cov` sorts comparison rows by lowest applicable allocation coverage first, using the current snapshot for added/changed rows and the old snapshot for removals. Zero-request rows, whose coverage is not applicable, sort last; shortage, requested date, change type, allocation unit, and order ID provide deterministic ties. Sort precedence is `p_wors`, `p_cw`, `p_spw`, `p_sreg`, `p_qd`, `p_cov`, `p_spct`, `p_due`, then `p_shrt`, and comparison contextual CSV/JSON schema is now `57`.

`p_spct` sorts comparison rows by highest applicable shortage percentage first, using the current snapshot for added/changed rows and the old snapshot for removals. Zero-request rows sort last; absolute shortage, requested date, change type, allocation unit, and order ID provide deterministic ties. Sort precedence is `p_wors`, `p_cov`, `p_spct`, `p_due`, then `p_shrt`.

Result `p_dcnt` orders rows by the persisted originating run's demand count, then shortage, requested date, allocation unit, priority, run ID, and order ID. Result `p_dage` orders rows by the persisted originating-run effective deadline: most overdue signed ages first, then deadline date, shortage, requested date, allocation unit, priority, run ID, and order ID; rows without an effective deadline remain last. Result `p_due` orders by the earliest effective originating-run deadline, then shortage, snapshot requested date, allocation unit, priority, run ID, and order ID; rows without an effective deadline remain last. Sort precedence is `p_pri`, `p_sstat`, `p_cov`, `p_spct`, `p_dcnt`, `p_dage`, `p_due`, `p_tdur`, `p_big`, `p_done`, `p_date`, `p_rdate`, then `p_shrt`.

Exact-run `ZSTOCK_ALLOC_RESULT` reconciliation applies the same requested-total equality in JSON metadata and human output, alongside its existing outcome, allocated, and shortage checks. JSON metadata and human output also expose `reconciliation_fields`, identifying the mismatched audit metrics or the state reason (`not_requested`, `filtered`, or `unavailable`).

Comparison contextual outputs additionally expose old/new snapshot full, partial, and unallocated row counts plus requested, allocated, and shortage totals. Exact-run result metadata and human output expose the corresponding snapshot metrics for direct audit diagnosis.

The following comparison schema history records release-time versions through schema `57`; later extensions are summarized in the current comparison contract below.

Schema history entry 36 added exact persisted requested-horizon guards and their filter provenance after version 35 added the effective requested-deadline fields. Version 34 adds old/new effective requested deadlines derived from each requested horizon. Version 33 adds typed and contextual overdue as-of filter provenance. Version 32 adds the boolean `overdue_only` value to typed JSON `filter_values` alongside the explicit `movement_type_filter` and `minimum_shelf_life_filter` fields. Version 31 adds overdue-only snapshot filtering and provenance. Version 30 adds explicit `movement_type_filter` and `minimum_shelf_life_filter` fields alongside the existing `filter_values` object. Version 29 adds exact `p_mvt` and `p_shelf` policy filters for old/new audit-run resolution and preserves their values in filter provenance. Version 28 adds old/new movement type and minimum shelf-life policy plus `movement_type` and `shelf_life` audit metadata-change reasons. Version 27 adds `filters_applied` and a stable `filters` array; version 25 adds `typed: true` to typed comparison rows and contextual JSON envelopes. Version 24 adds `has_previous`, nullable `previous_offset`, and nullable `page_number` page-navigation metadata alongside the forward-navigation fields. Version 23 adds `has_more` and nullable `next_offset` page-navigation metadata. Version 22 adds query scope (`material`, `plant`, `storage_location`, `batch`, and `unit`) to contextual output. Version 21 adds a numeric `filter_values` object containing `offset` and `max_rows` (`null` when the page is unbounded), represented as a quoted JSON object in contextual CSV. Version 20 adds `audit_running_age_trend` (`older`, `younger`, `unchanged`, or `unavailable`) beside the live `old_running_age_seconds`, `new_running_age_seconds`, and `audit_running_age_delta_seconds` fields introduced in version 19. Typed JSON emits numbers when the corresponding run is active with usable timestamps and `null` otherwise; regular JSON and CSV use numeric text or `n/a`. Version 18 adds `audit_reconciliation_transition`, classified as `both_ok`, `recovered`, `regressed`, `both_mismatch`, or `unavailable`. Version 17's `audit_metadata_change_reasons`, a pipe-delimited list of changed audit metadata categories (`status`, `strategy`, `unit`, `movement_type`, `shelf_life`, `horizon`, `timestamps`, and `message`), version 16's aggregate metadata flag, and version 15's `audit_reconciliation_ok` health flag remain available alongside the reconciliation statuses, transition indicator, outcome-counter deltas, timing deltas, running-state and duration deltas, status/strategy indicators, requested-delivery-horizon changes, unit-safe quantity deltas, and coverage/shortage-percentage deltas. Timing and quantity deltas retain typed `null` and regular `n/a` when their inputs are unavailable or non-comparable. Unit-safe audit coverage and shortage percentages remain available per run, with zero-request runs represented as `null` in typed JSON and `n/a` elsewhere. These complement audit-side outcome counters and requested/allocated/shortage totals alongside persisted start/finish times, generated date/time, run duration, requested-delivery horizon, and available-stock baseline context. Snapshot-side and audit-side metrics are both available for direct reconciliation diagnosis. Plain JSON arrays and NDJSON remain row-only for backward compatibility; contextual CSV and JSON expose the same page count and final-page offset.
Earlier comparison contextual contracts through schema `57` added independent old/new audit diagnostic filters, lifecycle filters, allocation-status filters, and the comparison sort modes through shortage-percentage worsening. Those historical contracts remain backward-compatible; the current schema and subsequent extensions are documented below.

Typed comparison rows and contextual JSON envelopes include boolean `typed: true`; default comparison rows retain their existing string-valued shape.

Comparison accepts `p_mvt` for an exact originating allocation movement type and nonnegative `p_shelf` for an exact minimum-shelf-life policy. Both filters constrain old and new audit-run resolution, are reported in human output and filter provenance, and advance contextual comparison contracts through schemas `29` and `30`; a mismatch returns an explicit policy-filter diagnostic.

`p_latest` selects the newest matching audit run automatically and applies that run ID to the result read, JSON metadata, reconciliation, and CSV output. When supplied, `p_from` and `p_to` also constrain the audit match to the requested-delivery horizon, while `p_covf`/`p_covt` and `p_spf`/`p_spt` constrain the aggregate run coverage and shortage-percentage metrics used to select that latest run. It cannot be combined with an explicit `p_runid`; use the exact run ID when a specific historical run is required.

`p_strat` restricts result rows and `p_latest` selection to persisted strategy `P`, `F`, `N`, `S`, `L`, `B`, `E`, `A`, or `W`; `p_legacy` restricts them to blank legacy strategy runs. These strategy filters are mutually exclusive and are included in metadata filter names.

`p_rage` optionally keeps only reserved rows whose reservation date is at least the supplied number of days old; `p_rageto` optionally adds a maximum age so the result is restricted to an inclusive reservation-age window. Negative ages and reversed ranges are rejected, and unreserved rows are excluded when either bound is active.

`p_rid` optionally keeps result rows linked to a run ID containing the supplied fragment, case-insensitively. It can be combined with an exact `p_runid` for an additional guard, and it is applied when `p_latest` selects the newest matching run.

For any filtered result set, the report prints total lines and counts of fully allocated, partially allocated, and unallocated demands before the detailed rows. It also prints requested/allocated/shortage quantity totals when all rows share one allocation unit; mixed-unit result sets intentionally omit quantity aggregation.

Result-line `p_covf`/`p_covt` filters select inclusive allocation-coverage percentages from 0 to 100, calculated as allocated quantity divided by requested quantity. Lines with zero requested quantity are excluded when coverage bounds are supplied. `p_spf`/`p_spt` apply the complementary shortage-percentage range, calculated as shortage quantity divided by requested quantity; lines with zero requested quantity are excluded when those bounds are supplied. `p_spct` orders rows by descending shortage percentage, then shortage, requested date, allocation unit, priority, and order key.

Selecting result-report `p_cov` orders lines by ascending allocation coverage, then descending shortage, requested date, allocation unit, priority, and order key. Sort precedence is priority (`p_pri`), coverage (`p_cov`), requested date (`p_date`), then shortage (`p_shrt`).

Result-report `p_max` caps the returned rows after filtering and sorting; a positive value is required for a cap, negative values are rejected before the read, and capped exact-run views do not claim full reconciliation. Exact run-ID reads are pushed into the SAP SELECT predicate for a narrower database read.

Result-report `p_skip` skips that many rows after filtering and sorting, before `p_max`; it must be nonnegative, and any nonzero offset disables exact-run reconciliation.

Selecting result-report `p_big` orders by descending requested quantity, then descending shortage, requested date, allocation unit, priority, and order key. Sort precedence is priority, coverage, largest demand (`p_big`), requested date, then shortage.

Selecting result-report `p_dcnt` orders rows by the persisted originating run's demand count, then shortage, requested date, allocation unit, priority, run ID, and order key. It follows shortage-percentage ordering and precedes deadline-age, requested-deadline, audit-duration, quantity, date, reservation-date, and shortage ordering. Current result contracts use detail schema `40` and summary schema `46`.

Selecting `p_done` orders by descending allocated quantity, then requested quantity, shortage, requested date, allocation unit, priority, and order key. It follows `p_big` and precedes requested-date/shortage ordering.

Selecting result-report `p_rdate` orders by reservation date ascending, then allocation unit, priority, and order key; unreserved rows sort first so reservation follow-up is immediately visible. It follows requested-date ordering and precedes shortage ordering.

Selecting result-report `p_csv` emits only the filtered detail rows as semicolon-delimited output with a stable header, including generated date/time, numeric detail `schema_version` `39`, sort/filter state, a pipe-delimited `filters` field, explicit audit-status, preview provenance, message, message-only, movement-type, minimum-shelf-life, safety-stock, requested-horizon, audit-start-date, reservation-age, and deadline-only values, offset/limit, page navigation, `page_count`, `last_offset`, `total_rows` before pagination, material/plant/storage/batch scope, persisted strategy, coverage, shortage percentage, reservation provenance, exact-audit movement type, minimum shelf-life and safety-stock policies, effective requested deadline, and `audit_running_age_seconds` for an exact running audit selection (`n/a` otherwise); human-readable summaries and reconciliation text are intentionally omitted. Combining `p_sum` with `p_csv` instead emits one stable page-scoped summary row with generated date/time, numeric summary `schema_version` `45`, sort/filter state, a pipe-delimited `filters` field, explicit policy, preview, message, requested-horizon, audit-start-date, and reservation-age values, offset/limit, page navigation, `page_count`, `last_offset`, `total_rows`, aggregate snapshot `demand_count`, result-line counts, per-strategy result-line counts, strategy context, per-strategy requested/allocated/shortage/coverage totals, unit-safe quantity totals, aggregate `coverage_pct`, aggregate `shortage_pct`, and exact-audit policy/deadline/safety-stock context; mixed-unit totals and percentages are `n/a`.

CSV data fields are quoted and embedded quotes are doubled, so identifiers containing delimiters or quotes remain importable by standard CSV tools.

Numeric result CSV fields use a locale-independent period decimal separator with no thousands grouping; unavailable coverage remains `n/a`.

When result CSV validation, authorization, or retrieval fails, the report emits the shared `mode;status;schema_version;message` error envelope with `status` set to `error`; detail uses schema `40` and summary uses schema `46` instead of human-readable prose.

Selecting result-report `p_json` emits the same filtered detail rows as a JSON array with stable property names, including material/plant/storage/batch scope; human output also reports the total matching line count before pagination. JSON mode is mutually exclusive with `p_csv`, and validation, authorization, or read failures emit JSON error envelopes.

Result JSON errors use schema `46` in summary mode and `40` for detail, metadata-detail, or NDJSON modes.

Selecting result-report `p_ndjson` together with `p_json` emits one complete detail-row object per line for streaming consumers. It reuses the regular or typed row contract, emits no array delimiters, and is mutually exclusive with `p_meta` and `p_sum`; an empty page emits no data lines. It requires JSON mode.

Selecting result-report `p_typed` with `p_json` emits numeric priority, quantities, coverage, shortage percentage, and minimum-shelf-life filter values directly in each row; non-applicable percentages are `null`. Typed detail rows carry schema version `40`, persisted strategy, generated date/time, material/plant/storage/batch scope, sort/filter state, page-navigation context, explicit audit-status, preview provenance, message, message-only, movement-type, requested-horizon, audit start/finish-date, reservation-age, and deadline-only values, numeric/null minimum-shelf-life filter, `page_count`, `last_offset`, `total_rows`, and exact-audit movement type, minimum shelf-life, and safety-stock policies, effective requested deadline, plus `audit_running_age_seconds` (`null` when not applicable). Typed summaries use schema version `46` and additionally expose per-strategy result-line counts, strategy context, numeric per-strategy quantity/coverage totals, numeric aggregate coverage/shortage percentages, explicit policy, message, requested-horizon, audit start/finish-date, and reservation-age values, and exact-audit policy/deadline/safety-stock context when a run is selected. Typed detail rows also expose numeric `filter_values` bounds with `null` for blank filters. It is mutually exclusive with `p_meta`, which provides the equivalent typed values in metadata envelopes.

Typed result detail rows and detail metadata envelopes use numeric `schema_version: 40`; typed result summaries and summary metadata envelopes use numeric `schema_version: 46`. Detail rows expose the persisted strategy, row-level `coverage_pct` and `shortage_pct`, explicit audit-status, preview provenance, message, message-only, policy, requested-horizon, audit start/finish-date, and reservation-age filter values including boolean `requested_deadline_only`, exact-audit `audit_movement_type`, numeric `audit_min_shelf_life` and `audit_safety_stock`, effective `audit_requested_deadline`, signed `audit_deadline_age_days`, `deadline_age_reference_date`, and `audit_running_age_seconds` (`null` unless the exact audit is currently running); summaries expose explicit snapshot `demand_count` and per-strategy result-line counts, strategy context (`P`, `F`, `N`, `S`, `L`, `B`, `legacy`, `mixed`, or `n/a`), per-strategy requested/allocated/shortage/coverage totals, aggregate `coverage_pct`/`shortage_pct`, and exact-audit policy/deadline/safety-stock context when available. Mixed-unit or zero-request summaries retain `n/a` for unavailable quantity percentages, while non-applicable detail percentages are `null` in typed output. Typed summaries include material/plant/storage/batch scope. Both shapes include boolean `typed: true` and numeric `filter_values` objects for machine-readable bounds, including persisted requested-horizon, audit start/finish-date, and reservation-age bounds, generated date/time, sort/filter state, page-navigation context, `page_count`, `last_offset`, and `total_rows`. Exact-run JSON metadata additionally exposes the persisted `audit_strategy`, `audit_movement_type`, numeric `audit_min_shelf_life` and `audit_safety_stock`, effective `audit_requested_deadline`, signed `audit_deadline_age_days`, `deadline_age_reference_date`, numeric `audit_duration_seconds`, and live `audit_running_age_seconds` (JSON `null` when not applicable); human-readable exact-run context labels blank legacy strategies as `LEGACY` and prints strategy, movement type, minimum shelf-life, safety stock, effective deadline, duration, live age, and the reference date.

Selecting `p_meta` together with `p_json` wraps detail output in an object containing `schema_version`, `generated_date`, `generated_time`, effective `sort`, `filters_applied`, a `filters` string array, query scope (`material`, `plant`, `storage_location`, `batch`, `run_id`), `row_count`, `page_count`, `last_offset`, `total_rows`, `offset`, `max_rows`, `page_number`, `has_previous`, `previous_offset`, and the `rows` array; with an exact `p_runid`, it also includes persisted audit lifecycle status, strategy, movement type, minimum shelf-life policy, message, start/finish timestamps, counters, quantities, and `reconciliation` (`OK`, `MISMATCH`, `filtered`, or `unavailable`). Without `p_meta`, the backward-compatible JSON array is retained.

`p_meta` requires `p_json`; it is rejected when JSON output is not selected. Metadata query scope includes both `run_id` for exact matching and `run_id_contains` for fragment matching.

Metadata pagination fields, including `page_count`, `last_offset`, and `total_rows`, and audit counters/quantities are emitted as JSON numbers; `has_more` is a JSON boolean indicating that another page exists, `next_offset` points to the following page, and `last_offset` points to the final bounded page. `last_offset` is `0` for an empty bounded result and JSON `null` for unbounded reads. Each metadata detail row also includes a `numeric` object for machine-readable priority, quantities, coverage, and other row metrics; the original flat detail fields retain their existing string encoding. Non-applicable numeric row metrics are JSON `null`.

Numeric metadata uses locale-independent JSON formatting, including a period decimal separator and no thousands grouping.

In metadata summary envelopes, summary counts, quantities, and numeric coverage are also JSON numbers; the nested summary includes an explicit `mixed_units` boolean, while mixed-unit or unavailable values remain the string `n/a`.

The metadata `sort` value is `default` when no ordering checkbox is selected and otherwise names the first sort criterion in the
