documented precedence order. `filters_applied` is true when any optional query filter is active, and `filters` lists the active filter names in stable report order. `page_number` is one-based when `p_max` is positive and JSON `null` for unbounded reads; `has_previous` reflects a nonzero offset and `previous_offset` points to the prior bounded page or is `null` for unbounded reads.

Empty result or history detail JSON selections emit `[]`; with `p_meta`, they emit the same empty metadata wrapper with zero rows and pagination fields. Selecting `p_sum` emits a zero-count summary instead, with strategy context `n/a` and non-applicable quantity totals.

For bounded result and history reads, `last_offset` identifies the final page using the same offset unit as `p_skip`; it is `0` when no matching rows exist and JSON `null` for unbounded reads. Human output also prints the final-page offset beside the page count.

Selecting `p_bklg` keeps only lines with positive shortage, combining partial and unallocated demand for backlog follow-up; capped or filtered exact-run views do not claim full reconciliation.

Selecting result-report `p_ovrd` keeps only lines whose effective requested deadline is before the selected as-of date (today by default); blank dates and today/future dates are excluded, and the filtered view is not treated as an exact-run reconciliation.

Selecting result-report `p_unrsv` keeps only lines without a reservation ID for reservation follow-up; it is mutually exclusive with `p_rsv` and filtered views do not claim exact-run reconciliation.

History `p_csv` emits one semicolon-delimited row per filtered run with generated date/time, numeric detail `schema_version` `26`, sort/filter state, a pipe-delimited `filters` field, explicit `movement_type_filter`, `minimum_shelf_life_filter`, and `requested_deadline_only` values, offset/limit, page navigation, `page_count`, `last_offset`, `total_rows` before pagination, scope, movement type, minimum shelf-life and safety-stock policies, unit, persisted strategy, requested horizon, quantities, row-level coverage and shortage percentage, lifecycle status, diagnostic message, outcome counts, and start/finish timestamps. Running rows additionally expose live `running_age_seconds`; finalized rows use `n/a`; human-readable summary text is omitted. Combining `p_sum` with `p_csv` instead emits one stable page-scoped summary row with generated date/time, numeric summary `schema_version` `43`, sort/filter state, a pipe-delimited `filters` field, explicit policy filter values, offset/limit, page navigation, `page_count`, `last_offset`, `total_rows`, strategy context (`P`, `F`, `N`, `S`, `L`, `B`, `legacy`, `mixed`, or `n/a`) derived from the returned page, movement-type, minimum-shelf-life, and safety-stock policy context (`n/a`, one value, or `mixed`), deadline_count, earliest/latest effective requested deadlines, per-strategy run counts, run-status counts including `completion_pct`, `success_rate_pct`, `partial_rate_pct`, and `error_rate_pct`, per-strategy requested/allocated/shortage/coverage totals, outcome counts, aggregate `demand_count`, unit-safe aggregate quantities, aggregate `coverage_pct` and `shortage_pct`, `average_duration_seconds`, `minimum_duration_seconds`, and `maximum_duration_seconds` across completed runs, `completed_duration_runs`, `oldest_running_age_seconds`, `oldest_running_run_id`, `newest_running_age_seconds`, and `newest_running_run_id` for the active-run age range; mixed-unit totals are `n/a`.

Numeric history CSV fields use the same locale-independent period decimal separator and no thousands grouping; unavailable coverage and running durations remain `n/a`.

When history CSV validation, authorization, or retrieval fails, the report emits the shared `mode;status;schema_version;message` error envelope with `status` set to `error`; detail uses schema `26` and summary uses schema `43` instead of human-readable prose.

History `p_json` emits the same filtered runs as a JSON array with stable properties, including each ¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬Ãƒâ€¦Ã‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬Ãƒâ€¦Ã‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â¦ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬Ãƒâ€¦Ã‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¦ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬Ãƒâ€¦Ã‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¾Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¾Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â¦Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¦ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¾Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬Ãƒâ€¦Ã‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â¦ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â¦Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬Ãƒâ€¦Ã‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢s diagnostic message, and is mutually exclusive with history `p_csv`; validation, authorization, or read failures emit JSON error envelopes.

History JSON errors use schema `43` in summary mode and `26` for detail, metadata-detail, or NDJSON modes.

Selecting history `p_typed` with `p_json` emits numeric quantities, coverage, shortage percentage, outcome counts, duration, live running age, and minimum shelf-life policy directly in each row; non-applicable coverage, shortage percentage, duration, or running age is `null`. Typed detail rows also carry schema version `26`, generated date/time, material/plant/storage/batch scope, movement type, explicit `movement_type_filter` and numeric/null `minimum_shelf_life_filter`, sort/filter state, page-navigation context, `page_count`, `last_offset`, and `total_rows`. Typed detail rows also expose numeric `filter_values` bounds with `null` for blank filters. It is mutually exclusive with history `p_meta`.

Typed history detail rows and detail metadata envelopes use numeric `schema_version: 26`; typed history summaries and summary metadata envelopes use numeric `schema_version: 43`. Typed summaries include material/plant/storage/batch scope, explicit `movement_type_filter` and numeric/null `minimum_shelf_life_filter`, explicit `safety_stock_filter` with numeric/null `minimum_safety_stock_filter` and `maximum_safety_stock_filter`, strategy context (`P`, `F`, `N`, `S`, `L`, `B`, `legacy`, `mixed`, or `n/a`) derived from the returned page, movement-type context, earliest/latest effective requested deadlines, numeric or nullable minimum-shelf-life and safety-stock contexts, per-strategy run counts, numeric `completion_pct`, `success_rate_pct`, `partial_rate_pct`, and `error_rate_pct`, numeric aggregate `demand_count`, numeric `deadline_count`, unit-safe per-strategy requested/allocated/shortage/coverage totals, numeric aggregate `coverage_pct` and `shortage_pct`, numeric `average_duration_seconds`, `minimum_duration_seconds`, and `maximum_duration_seconds` across completed runs, plus numeric `completed_duration_runs`, `oldest_running_age_seconds`, and `newest_running_age_seconds` (`null` when no running rows), with `oldest_running_run_id` and `newest_running_run_id` as `n/a` when no active run is available, while detail rows also expose movement type, minimum shelf-life and safety-stock policies, persisted strategy, row-level `coverage_pct`, row-level `shortage_pct`, and `running_age_seconds` (`null` for finalized rows). Mixed-unit summaries retain `n/a` for all quantity totals. Both shapes include boolean `typed: true` and typed/metadata JSON exposes numeric `filter_values` with `null` for blank numeric bounds, generated date/time, sort/filter state, page-navigation context, `page_count`, `last_offset`, and `total_rows`.

Selecting history `p_meta` together with `p_json` wraps detail output in an object containing `schema_version`, `generated_date`, `generated_time`, effective `sort`, `filters_applied`, a `filters` string array, query scope (`material`, `plant`, `storage_location`, `batch`, `run_id`), `mode`, `row_count`, `page_count`, `last_offset`, `total_rows`, `offset`, `max_rows`, `page_number`, `has_previous`, `previous_offset`, and the `rows` array; an exact `p_runid` also includes persisted audit lifecycle status, message, start/finish timestamps, counters, and quantities. With `p_sum`, the same envelope contains a nested `summary` object instead of `rows`, including movement-type and minimum-shelf-life policy context, and retains the exact-run audit lifecycle context; without `p_meta`, summary mode continues to emit its existing compact summary object.

History `p_meta` requires `p_json`; it is rejected when JSON output is not selected.

Selecting history `p_ndjson` together with `p_json` emits one complete run object per line for streaming consumers. It reuses the regular or typed row contract, emits no array delimiters, and is mutually exclusive with `p_meta` and `p_sum`; an empty page emits no data lines. It requires JSON mode.

History metadata pagination fields, including `page_count`, `last_offset`, and `total_rows`, and audit counters/quantities are emitted as JSON numbers; `has_more` is a JSON boolean indicating that another page exists, `next_offset` points to the following page, and `last_offset` points to the final bounded page. `last_offset` is `0` for an empty bounded history result and JSON `null` for unbounded reads. Each metadata detail row also includes a `numeric` object for machine-readable quantities, coverage, outcome counts, and duration; the original flat detail fields retain their existing string encoding. Non-applicable numeric row metrics are JSON `null`.

History numeric metadata uses locale-independent JSON formatting, including a period decimal separator and no thousands grouping.

In history metadata summary envelopes, summary counts, quantities, numeric `completion_pct`, `success_rate_pct`, `partial_rate_pct`, `error_rate_pct`, coverage, `shortage_pct`, `average_duration_seconds`, `minimum_duration_seconds`, `maximum_duration_seconds`, and `completed_duration_runs` are JSON numbers; `oldest_running_age_seconds` and `newest_running_age_seconds` are numeric, while `oldest_running_run_id` and `newest_running_run_id` are strings, with both using `n/a` when no active run is available (or JSON `null` for typed/non-applicable summary age).

The history metadata `sort` value is `default` when no ordering checkbox is selected and otherwise names the first sort criterion in the documented precedence order. History `filters_applied` is true when any optional query filter is active, and `filters` lists the active filter names in stable report order. History `page_number` is one-based when `p_max` is positive and JSON `null` for unbounded reads; `has_previous` reflects a nonzero offset and `previous_offset` points to the prior bounded page or is `null` for unbounded reads.

History `p_cov` orders runs by ascending allocation coverage, then descending shortage, newest start chronology, and run ID; it takes precedence over history `p_shrt` when both are selected. History `p_spct` orders runs by descending shortage percentage, then shortage, newest start chronology, and run ID. History `p_dcnt` orders runs by descending demand count, then shortage, newest start chronology, and run ID. History `p_dage` orders effective deadlines by descending signed age (most overdue first), then deadline date, shortage, newest start chronology, and run ID; runs without an effective deadline remain last. Coverage sorting takes precedence over shortage percentage, demand count, deadline age, requested date, status, duration, and shortage. History `p_spf`/`p_spt` calculate shortage percentage from shortage divided by allocated plus shortage and exclude runs with no requested quantity.

History and result detail rows include a calculated Coverage column and a complementary Shortage % column; rows with no requested quantity display `n/a`.

Direct audit summary responses also expose `movement_type_context`, `min_shelf_life_context`, `policy_context_available`, and `mixed_policies`; mixed policy populations keep the numeric shelf-life context initial and set the explicit mixed flag.

History accepts `p_mvt` for an exact persisted movement-type filter and `p_shelf` for a nonnegative minimum-shelf-life policy filter; `p_shelf = 0` leaves the shelf-life filter inactive. Both filters participate in the stable filter-name provenance, are exposed explicitly as `movement_type_filter` and `minimum_shelf_life_filter`, and typed/metadata JSON also exposes `movement_type` and `minimum_shelf_life` in `filter_values`.

Selecting `p_sum` in either read-only report keeps the filtered totals and status metrics while suppressing detail rows; result-report exact-run context and reconciliation output are still shown. In CSV mode, it selects the corresponding one-row summary schema.

Combining `p_sum` with `p_json` emits one compact summary object containing counts and unit-safe quantity totals instead of detail arrays; it includes an explicit `mixed_units` boolean, and mixed-unit totals are reported as `n/a`. When `p_meta` is also selected, the summary is nested under `summary` beside the same scope and pagination metadata used for detail pages, and an exact `p_runid` includes the persisted audit lifecycle context; without `p_meta`, the compact summary contract is unchanged.

History `p_latest` keeps only the newest matching run after all history filters and deterministic sorting; it is compatible with summary and export modes, but cannot be combined with a nonzero `p_skip` offset. `p_max` caps returned runs after filtering and sorting; a negative value is rejected.

History `p_skip` skips that many runs after filtering and sorting, before `p_max`; it must be nonnegative.
The allocation service API exports the created audit run ID immediately after `START_RUN`, including when a later reservation or persistence step raises, so integration callers can correlate failures without a history lookup.
When a post-start allocation failure is returned, `ZSTOCK_ALLOCATE` includes the correlated `run_id` in JSON errors, CSV error headers/rows (`mode;status;schema_version;message;run_id`), and human output; pre-start validation errors use the same versioned envelope without `run_id`.
Successful `ZSTOCK_ALLOCATE` output includes `run_id` for the allocation just performed and retains `last_run_id` plus `last_strategy` as the latest-run values from the independently read scope summary; the distinction prevents concurrent allocations from being misidentified and makes the latest-run context self-describing. Aggregate coverage is calculated from unit-safe requested and allocated totals; it remains zero-safe and unavailable when the summary mixes units, while `shortage_pct` is the complementary aggregate shortage percentage. `completion_pct` is the share of all scoped audit runs that have finalized success, partial, or error status, while `success_rate_pct`, `partial_rate_pct`, and `error_rate_pct` partition the finalized population. `last_duration_seconds` is calculated from the selected latest run's persisted start and finish timestamps, while `average_duration_seconds`, `minimum_duration_seconds`, and `maximum_duration_seconds` cover completed runs in the scoped population and exclude running rows; `completed_duration_runs` reports the denominator. `oldest_running_age_seconds`/`oldest_running_run_id` report the oldest active audit run, while `newest_running_age_seconds`/`newest_running_run_id` report the newest active audit run; all remain non-applicable when no active run has a usable start timestamp.
Successful allocation lifecycle fields are refreshed from the exact returned run when available, so concurrent scope activity cannot replace the current ¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬Ãƒâ€¦Ã‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬Ãƒâ€¦Ã‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â¦ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬Ãƒâ€¦Ã‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¦ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬Ãƒâ€¦Ã‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¾Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¾Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â¦Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¦ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¾Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬Ãƒâ€¦Ã‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â¦ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¬ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Â¦Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™Ãƒâ€ Ã¢â‚¬â„¢ÃƒÆ’Ã‚Â¢ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ã‚Â¬Ãƒâ€¦Ã‚Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã†â€™ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…Â¡ÃƒÆ’Ã¢â‚¬Å¡Ãƒâ€šÃ‚Â¢s status, message, requested window, or timestamps in the response.
Human-readable allocation failures include the same `Run ID` line even when the fallback audit-summary read fails.
Direct audit `get_runs` records now expose derived `requested` quantity alongside allocated and shortage, calculated after persisted-row validation without adding a database column.
`ZSTOCK_ALLOCATE` accepts `p_strat` (`P` by default for priority-first, `F` for FIFO by requested delivery date, `N` for full-only, `S` for smallest-demand-first, `L` for largest-demand-first, `B` for best-fit, `E` for fair-share, `A` for adaptive, or `W` for weighted fair-share). FIFO uses priority and order ID as deterministic tie-breakers. Smallest-demand-first sorts by requested quantity ascending, then priority, requested date, and order ID; largest-demand-first uses the same deterministic tie-breakers with requested quantity descending. Best-fit prefers exact fits, then candidates that leave another demand fitting the remaining stock, then deterministic largest-demand and priority/date/order tie-breakers; it falls back to the smallest deterministic candidate when no demand fits. Full-only skips a demand when its entire requested quantity does not fit, preserving stock for later lines that can be completely fulfilled. Fair-share distributes scarce stock deterministically across active demands, adaptive selects priority when all demand fits and fair-share otherwise, and weighted fair-share distributes scarce stock by normalized priority weight. `p_shg` enables a maximum-shortage guard using `p_shmax`; when the calculated shortage exceeds the cap, the service records a rejected audit row and exits before reservations or snapshot writes. `p_covg` enables a minimum-coverage guard using `p_covmin` from 0 through 100 percent; when aggregate coverage is below the threshold, the service records a rejected audit row and exits before reservations or snapshot writes. `p_fullg` enables a minimum full-line guard using `p_fmin` from 0 through 100 percent; when the percentage of fully allocated demand lines is below the threshold, the service records a rejected audit row and exits before reservations or snapshot writes. `p_flg`/`p_flmin` enable a minimum full-line-count guard, while `p_mflg`/`p_mflmax` enable a maximum full-line-count guard; both reject calculated scopes before audit start or reservation side effects. `p_dg` enables a maximum-demand guard using nonnegative `p_dmax`; when the number of open demand lines exceeds the cap, the service records a rejected audit row and exits before allocation side effects. `p_qg` enables a maximum-requested-quantity guard using nonnegative `p_qmax` in the normalized allocation unit after demand-unit conversion; exceeded scopes are audited and rejected before allocation side effects. `p_ag` enables a maximum-allocated-quantity guard using nonnegative `p_amax` in the normalized allocation unit; exceeded calculated allocations are audited and rejected before reservation or snapshot side effects. `p_lg` enables a maximum-allocated-line guard using nonnegative `p_lmax`; exceeded positive-allocation lines are audited and rejected before audit start or reservation side effects. `p_spg` enables a maximum-shortage-percentage guard using `p_spmax` from 0 through 100 percent; exceeded aggregate shortage percentages are audited and rejected before audit start or reservation side effects. `p_ug` enables a maximum-unallocated-line guard using nonnegative `p_umax`; exceeded unallocated demand lines are audited and rejected before audit start or reservation side effects. `p_pg` enables a maximum-partial-line guard using nonnegative `p_pmax`; exceeded partial allocations are audited and rejected before audit start or reservation side effects. `p_mg` enables a minimum-allocation guard using nonnegative `p_mmin`; insufficient calculated allocation is audited and rejected before audit start or reservation side effects. `p_ilg` enables a minimum-allocation-line guard using nonnegative `p_imin`; scopes with too few fully or partially allocated demand lines are audited and rejected before audit start or reservation side effects. `p_slg` enables a maximum-shortage-line guard using nonnegative `p_slmax`; scopes with too many partial or unallocated demand lines are audited and rejected before audit start or reservation side effects. Successful CSV and JSON allocation contracts now use schema version `49`, include the normalized strategy name, movement type, minimum shelf-life policy, entered guard provenance and requested-date filter, `completion_pct`, `success_rate_pct`, `partial_rate_pct`, `error_rate_pct`, unit-safe aggregate requested/allocated/shortage/coverage totals, aggregate `shortage_pct`, full/partial/unallocated line counts, aggregate `demand_count`, per-strategy run counters and requested/allocated/shortage/coverage totals, latest-run and minimum/average/maximum completed-run durations with the completed-run denominator, and the latest-run persisted strategy beside `last_run_id`.

The selected strategy is also persisted on the audit run as `P`, `F`, `N`, `S`, `L`, `B`, `E`, `A`, or `W`, and is returned by run history and summary APIs; legacy runs created before this field existed may have a blank strategy. Human-readable history labels those blank values as `LEGACY`; CSV and JSON preserve the blank persisted value.
Audit summaries additionally expose `priority_runs`, `fifo_runs`, `full_only_runs`, `smallest_runs`, `largest_runs`, `best_runs`, `fair_runs`, `weighted_runs`, `adaptive_runs`, `adaptive_priority_runs`, `adaptive_fair_runs`, and `legacy_strategy_runs` counters.
The direct audit `get_summary` API accepts the same optional `iv_legacy_strategy`, `iv_message_contains`, `iv_message_only`, requested-delivery horizon, start/finish date-window, duration-range, stale-running, maximum-running-age, coverage, shortage-percentage, shortage-quantity, allocated-quantity, available-stock, derived-requested-quantity, and demand-count filters as `get_runs`, plus exact `iv_run_id`, case-insensitive `iv_run_id_contains`, and lifecycle `iv_status` filters; it cannot be combined with `iv_strategy` and summarizes only the selected population. Summaries also expose aggregate `demand_count` and `deadline_count` across the selected runs. Unit-consistent summaries expose aggregate requested, allocated, shortage, zero-safe coverage, and `shortage_pct` values plus per-strategy requested, allocated, shortage, and coverage percentages for priority, FIFO, full-only, smallest, largest, best-fit, and legacy runs; all quantity, aggregate coverage, shortage percentage, and per-strategy coverage fields are cleared when the population mixes units. `completion_pct` is independent of allocation unit and measures finalized statuses across the selected runs; `success_rate_pct`, `partial_rate_pct`, and `error_rate_pct` partition the finalized population. Summary duration metrics are also independent of allocation unit: latest duration follows the deterministic newest run, minimum/average/maximum duration cover completed runs, and `completed_duration_runs` reports the average denominator. `oldest_running_age_seconds` and `newest_running_age_seconds` are calculated from the current timestamp for active runs with valid start timestamps. The audit `get_running_age` API accepts optional reference date/time inputs for deterministic replay and boundary testing; omitted inputs use the current system clock.

`ZSTOCK_ALLOC_HISTORY` accepts `p_strat` (`P`, `F`, `N`, `S`, `L`, `B`, `E`, `A`, or `W`) to restrict history output to one persisted strategy.
Use `p_legacy` to select only runs with a blank persisted strategy from before strategy persistence; it cannot be combined with `p_strat`.
History detail CSV and JSON rows include the persisted strategy and use the current detail schema version `26`.
Human-readable history output now prints page-derived strategy context and per-strategy run counts in summaries, plus a strategy column for each run; a blank-only page is labeled `legacy`, a mixed page is labeled `mixed`, and an empty summary page is labeled `n/a`.

`ZSTOCK_ALLOC_RESULT` accepts `p_strat` (`P`, `F`, `N`, `S`, `L`, `B`, `E`, `A`, or `W`) to restrict snapshot rows to runs persisted with one allocation strategy, or `p_legacy` to select blank-strategy runs from before strategy persistence; the two filters cannot be combined. `p_astat` independently restricts originating audit lifecycle status to `R`, `S`, `P`, or `E`; `p_prev` independently restricts persisted preview (`P`) or operational (`O`) runs; `p_msg` applies a case-insensitive substring filter to the persisted audit message and `p_monly` keeps only runs with a nonblank message; `p_stat` remains the snapshot-line status filter (`F`, `P`, or `U`). `p_mvt` filters by the originating allocation movement type and nonnegative `p_shelf` filters by the persisted minimum shelf-life policy; `p_rmov` remains the separate reservation movement-type filter. `p_reqf`/`p_until` select the exact persisted audit requested-delivery horizon, `p_sfrom`/`p_sto` select an inclusive persisted audit lifecycle start-date window, `p_ffrom`/`p_fto` select an inclusive persisted audit lifecycle finish-date window, while `p_from`/`p_to` remain snapshot-row requested-date filters. `p_rfrom`/`p_rto` select reservation posting dates and `p_rage`/`p_rageto` select an inclusive reservation-age window. `p_dfrom`/`p_dto` select an inclusive persisted originating-run demand-count range, while `p_avf`/`p_avt` select an inclusive persisted originating-run available-stock range and `p_tfrom`/`p_tto` select an inclusive completed originating-run audit-duration range in seconds; `p_latest` applies all selected audit-policy, audit-status, preview, message, strategy, demand-count, available-stock, requested-horizon, audit-start-date, audit-finish-date, deadline-only, overdue/as-of, and reservation-age filters when choosing the newest matching audit run. Filter names and policy values are exposed in human, CSV, JSON, and NDJSON output; result detail exports use schema version `40` and include row-level `shortage_pct`, exact-audit movement type, minimum shelf-life and safety-stock policies, effective requested deadline, signed audit deadline age, audit start/finish-date and reservation-age provenance, and explicit filter values, while result summary exports use schema version `46` and include explicit aggregate `demand_count`, aggregate `shortage_pct`, best-fit line counts and totals, and exact-audit policy/deadline/safety-stock context.

The current `ZSTOCK_ALLOC_WATCH` contract adds `p_reqf`/`p_until` requested-delivery horizon filters, inclusive persisted audit lifecycle start-date bounds `p_from`/`p_to`, inclusive persisted audit lifecycle finish-date bounds `p_ffrom`/`p_fto`, `p_ovrd` overdue-horizon filtering, `p_odate` overdue as-of selection, `p_dead` deadline-bearing selection, `p_deadf`/`p_deadt` effective deadline-range filtering, `p_dagef`/`p_daget`/`p_daged` signed deadline-age filtering, `p_dcnt` demand-count-first ordering, `p_dage` deadline-age-first ordering, and `p_due` earliest-requested-date ordering. `p_ovrd` uses the delivery deadline (`requested_on_to`, otherwise `requested_on_from`) and keeps only dates before the selected as-of date, defaulting to the SAP system date. With `p_dcnt`, alerts sort by descending demand count, then shortage, age, start timestamp, and run ID. With `p_dage`, effective-deadline alerts sort by descending signed age (most overdue first), then deadline date, shortage, age, start timestamp, and run ID; alerts without an effective deadline sort last. With `p_due`, horizon-bearing alerts sort by requested start date and then requested end date, shortage, age, and run ID; horizon-less alerts sort last. Watch detail alerts expose the persisted `requested_on_from` and `requested_on_to` dates, effective `requested_deadline`, signed `deadline_age_days` (positive overdue, negative remaining), `deadline_age_reference_date`, and `deadline_age_mixed` in human, CSV, JSON, typed JSON, and NDJSON output; summary outputs expose `deadline_count`, the page's earliest/latest effective deadline range, oldest/newest deadline age bounds, the same reference date, and the mixed-provenance flag; current versions are CSV `59` and JSON/NDJSON `62`.

The current `ZSTOCK_ALLOC_HISTORY` contract also accepts `p_ovrd`, `p_odate`, and `p_dead` to retain only runs with an overdue requested-delivery deadline as of the selected date, `p_deadf`/`p_deadt` to select an inclusive effective deadline range, `p_dagef`/`p_daget`/`p_daged` to select a signed effective-deadline age range and its reference date, `p_dage` to order persisted runs by descending signed deadline age, and `p_due` to order persisted runs by earliest requested-delivery horizon, with horizon-less runs last and shortage/start/run-ID tie-breakers. `p_safon` enables an inclusive nonnegative persisted safety-stock range using `p_saf` and `p_safto`; the switch is required even for an exact zero range so zero-policy runs can be selected explicitly. One-sided horizons use their `requested_on_from` date for the effective deadline. Detail rows expose the effective `requested_deadline` used by overdue, deadline-range, and deadline-age filtering, signed `deadline_age_days` (positive overdue, negative remaining), and `deadline_age_reference_date`; summary rows expose `deadline_count`, the earliest/latest effective deadlines, oldest/newest deadline-age bounds, and the exact reference date represented by the returned page. `p_dcnt` additionally prioritizes runs by descending demand count before shortage and deterministic start/run-ID ties. Current history detail and summary machine-readable schemas are `26` and `43` respectively; detail rows include persisted `safety_stock`, and summaries include `safety_stock_context` with numeric/null typed behavior for mixed policies.
One-sided requested-delivery horizons use their effective date for this ordering: the lower bound when present, otherwise the upper bound.
Comparison also accepts common `p_ffrom`/`p_fto` and side-specific `p_offrom`/`p_ofto`/`p_nffrom`/`p_nfto` inclusive persisted audit lifecycle finish-date windows. Reversed bounds and common-plus-side combinations are rejected, and effective finish-date values are propagated to both audit and snapshot reads.
`ZSTOCK_ALLOC_COMPARE` also accepts `p_ovrd` to compare only overdue requested-date snapshot lines from both runs, or `p_oovrd`/`p_novrd` to select overdue requested-date lines independently for the old/new sides. These are surfaced as overdue provenance fields; overdue filtering cannot be combined with strict reconciliation guard `p_guard`. `p_dead` compares only runs and snapshot rows with an effective requested deadline. Independent `p_omvt`/`p_nmvt` movement-type filters constrain the old/new originating audit runs separately, while common `p_mvt` remains available; common and side-specific movement filters cannot be combined. Independent `p_oshelf`/`p_nshelf` nonnegative minimum-shelf-life filters constrain old/new audit policies separately, while common `p_shelf` remains available; common and side-specific shelf-life filters cannot be combined. Independent `p_ounit`/`p_nunit` allocation-unit filters constrain old/new reads separately, while common `p_meins` remains available; common and side-specific unit filters cannot be combined. Independent `p_ormov`/`p_nrmov` reservation-movement filters constrain old/new snapshot reads separately, while common `p_rmov` remains available; movement filters are numeric and common and side-specific reservation-movement filters cannot be combined. Independent `p_orunit`/`p_nrunit` reservation-unit filters constrain old/new reads separately, while common `p_runit` remains available; common and side-specific reservation-unit filters cannot be combined. Common `p_rsv`/`p_unrsv` and side-specific `p_orsv`/`p_nrsv`/`p_oursv`/`p_nursv` reservation-state switches select reserved or unreserved rows, reject conflicting states and common-plus-side combinations, and are reported independently for each side. Common `p_rfrom`/`p_rto` and side-specific `p_orfrom`/`p_orto`/`p_nrfrom`/`p_nrto` reservation-date ranges select inclusive posting-date windows for old/new snapshots; reversed ranges and common-plus-side combinations are rejected. Common `p_rage`/`p_rageto` and side-specific `p_orage`/`p_oragto`/`p_nrage`/`p_nragto` nonnegative reservation-age bounds keep snapshot reservations inside inclusive age windows; reversed bounds and common-plus-side combinations are rejected, and age provenance is reported independently for each side. Independent `p_omsg`/`p_nmsg` case-insensitive message filters, `p_omonly`/`p_nmonly` nonblank-message filters, and `p_ostr`/`p_nstr` persisted-strategy filters constrain the old and new originating audit runs separately. `p_oleg`/`p_nleg` independently select blank-strategy legacy runs for the old/new sides and cannot be combined with the corresponding explicit strategy filter. Common `p_afrom`/`p_ato` and side-specific `p_oafrom`/`p_oato`/`p_nafrom`/`p_nato` inclusive audit lifecycle start-date windows constrain originating old/new runs; reversed bounds and common-plus-side combinations are rejected, and effective values are published in every comparison output.
The current comparison CSV and contextual JSON schemas are `98`; version 98 adds common and old/new side-specific audit lifecycle finish-date windows and effective provenance after version 97 added audit lifecycle start-date windows. Version 96 added common and old/new side-specific preview-versus-operational run-type filters, effective preview filter provenance, and selected old/new run preview flags. Detail fields `old_snapshot_coverage_pct`, `new_snapshot_coverage_pct`, `old_snapshot_shortage_pct`, and `new_snapshot_shortage_pct` are calculated from each row's allocated, shortage, and requested quantities; zero-request sides, mixed-unit summaries, and unavailable deltas remain `n/a` in CSV/human output and JSON `null` in typed JSON.
Comparison accepts `p_prev` for a common preview (`P`) or operational (`O`) run-type filter, or `p_oprev`/`p_nprev` for old/new run-type filters independently; values are case-insensitive, common and side-specific run-type filters cannot be combined, and effective old/new values are applied to both audit-run and snapshot selection. Selected run preview flags and common/effective side-specific filter values are carried through human, CSV, JSON, NDJSON, and typed filter provenance. It also accepts `p_avf`/`p_avt` for a common inclusive persisted available-stock range, or `p_oavf`/`p_oavt` and `p_navf`/`p_navt` for old/new ranges independently; bounds must be nonnegative and ordered, and common and side-specific ranges cannot be combined. Effective available-stock values are carried through audit selection, snapshot selection, and all comparison filter provenance. It also accepts `p_tfrom`/`p_tto` to constrain both completed persisted audit runs by inclusive elapsed duration in seconds, or `p_otfrom`/`p_otto` and `p_ntfrom`/`p_ntto` to constrain old and new durations independently; duration bounds use the same common-versus-side-specific validation. Deadline, age, strategy, policy, reservation, quantity, snapshot-date, audit-start-date, and audit-finish-date filters retain their existing independent old/new behavior, with current `sort_mode` metadata in schema `98`.

Comparison detail output now includes numeric/string `old_safety_stock` and `new_safety_stock` audit-policy values in CSV and JSON/NDJSON, and `audit_metadata_change_reasons` includes `safety_stock` when the policy changes between runs.

`p_rdate` sorts comparison rows by the earliest available reservation date, using the new snapshot for added/changed rows and the old snapshot for removals. Rows without a reservation date sort last; shortage, change type, allocation unit, and order ID provide deterministic ties. It has lower precedence than `p_due` and higher precedence than `p_shrt` when multiple comparison sort checkboxes are selected.
`p_big` sorts comparison rows by descending effective requested quantity, while `p_done` sorts by descending effective allocated quantity; added/changed rows use the new snapshot and removals use the old snapshot. Shortage, requested date, change type, allocation unit, and order ID provide deterministic ties. Sort precedence is `p_wors`, `p_cw`, `p_spw`, `p_sreg`, `p_qd`, `p_cov`, `p_spct`, `p_big`, `p_done`, `p_due`, `p_rdate`, then `p_shrt`.
`p_bklg` keeps only shortage-bearing rows on both sides; `p_obklg` and `p_nbklg` apply that selection independently to old and new snapshots. Common and side-specific shortage filters cannot be combined, and the effective boolean selections are included in every comparison output shape.
`p_shf`/`p_sht` select an inclusive common shortage-quantity range; `p_oshf`/`p_osht` and `p_nshf`/`p_nsht` apply old/new ranges independently. Bounds must be nonnegative and ordered, common and side-specific shortage ranges cannot be combined, and numeric values are preserved in typed filter provenance.
`p_covf`/`p_covt` and `p_ocovf`/`p_ocovt`/`p_ncovf`/`p_ncovt` select common or independent old/new coverage-percentage ranges. `p_spf`/`p_spt` and `p_ospf`/`p_ospt`/`p_nspf`/`p_nspt` do the same for shortage percentage. All percentage bounds must be within 0..100 and ordered; common and side-specific ranges cannot be combined, and effective numeric values are retained in typed filter provenance.
`p_qf`/`p_qt` and `p_oqf`/`p_oqt`/`p_nqf`/`p_nqt` select common or independent requested-quantity ranges. `p_af`/`p_at` and `p_oaf`/`p_oat`/`p_naf`/`p_nat` do the same for allocated quantity. Bounds must be nonnegative and ordered; common and side-specific ranges cannot be combined, and effective numeric values are retained in typed filter provenance.
`p_priof`/`p_priot` select a common priority range, while `p_opf`/`p_opt` and `p_npf`/`p_npt` constrain old and new priorities independently. Priority bounds must be nonnegative and ordered; common and side-specific ranges cannot be combined, and effective numeric values are retained in typed filter provenance.
`p_sdf`/`p_sdt` select a common snapshot-line requested-date window, while `p_osdf`/`p_osdt` and `p_nsdf`/`p_nsdt` constrain old and new snapshot lines independently. These are distinct from the persisted audit requested-horizon filters `p_reqf`/`p_until`; snapshot bounds are inclusive, reversed and common-plus-side combinations are rejected, and effective dates are retained in filter provenance.
Schema `91` exports include the common and effective old/new snapshot requested-date bounds, common and old/new plant scopes, common and old/new material scopes, common and old/new storage-location scopes, common and old/new batch scopes, sales-document identities, sales-document types, sales items, schedule lines, order units, order IDs, and reservation IDs alongside the existing audit requested-horizon provenance.
`p_charg` selects a common batch, while `p_obatch` and `p_nbatch` select old and new batches independently; common and side-specific batch filters cannot be combined, and side-specific selection requires both values.
`p_vbeln` selects a common sales document, while `p_ovbeln` and `p_nvbeln` constrain old and new snapshots independently. Common and side-specific sales-document filters cannot be combined; effective document identities are retained in human, CSV, JSON, NDJSON, and typed filter provenance.
`p_auart` selects a common sales-document type, while `p_oauart` and `p_nauart` constrain old and new snapshots independently. Common and side-specific sales-document-type filters cannot be combined; effective document types are retained in all comparison provenance.
`p_posnr` selects a common sales item, while `p_oposnr` and `p_nposnr` constrain old and new snapshots independently. Common and side-specific sales-item filters cannot be combined; effective items are retained in all comparison provenance.
`p_etenr` selects a common schedule line, while `p_oetenr` and `p_netenr` constrain old and new snapshots independently. Common and side-specific schedule-line filters cannot be combined; effective schedule lines are retained in all comparison provenance.
`p_ordun` selects a common order unit, while `p_oordun` and `p_nordun` constrain old and new snapshots independently. Common and side-specific order-unit filters cannot be combined; effective order units are retained in all comparison provenance.
`p_order` selects a common order ID, while `p_oorder` and `p_norder` constrain old and new snapshots independently. Common and side-specific order-ID filters cannot be combined; effective order IDs are retained in all comparison provenance.
`p_resid` selects a common reservation ID, while `p_oresid` and `p_nresid` constrain old and new snapshots independently. Common and side-specific reservation-ID filters cannot be combined; effective reservation IDs are retained in all comparison provenance.
Comparison `p_reqf`/`p_until` constrain both persisted audit-run selections to the exact requested-delivery window; `p_afrom`/`p_ato` apply a common inclusive audit lifecycle start-date window, while `p_oafrom`/`p_oato` and `p_nafrom`/`p_nato` apply old/new windows independently. `p_ffrom`/`p_fto` apply a common inclusive audit lifecycle finish-date window, while `p_offrom`/`p_ofto` and `p_nffrom`/`p_nfto` apply old/new finish-date windows independently. Reversed bounds and common-plus-side combinations are rejected, and selected values appear in human, CSV, JSON, NDJSON, and typed filter provenance.
For `p_ovrd`, subset metrics remain available but reconciliation is reported as `FILTERED` with transition `unavailable`.
When multiple history sort checkboxes are selected, coverage takes precedence over shortage percentage, demand count, requested-date urgency, status, duration, and shortage; this precedence is also reflected in the reported `sort_mode`.
The current `ZSTOCK_ALLOC_RESULT` contract exposes the `p_ovrd` selection as boolean `overdue_only` and `p_dead` as boolean `requested_deadline_only` in human, CSV, JSON, NDJSON, and typed `filter_values` provenance. `p_prev` exposes persisted preview versus operational run provenance in the same output modes. `p_safon` enables an inclusive nonnegative persisted safety-stock range using `p_saf`/`p_safto`, including explicit zero; the switch is required when bounds are supplied. Result detail schemas are `40` and summary schemas are `46`; `p_astat` filters persisted audit lifecycle status, `p_msg`/`p_monly` filter persisted audit diagnostics, `p_sfrom`/`p_sto` filter persisted audit start dates, `p_ffrom`/`p_fto` filter persisted audit finish dates, `p_rage`/`p_rageto` filter reservation age, `p_dcnt` prioritizes rows from high-demand-count originating runs, `p_dage` prioritizes most overdue originating deadlines, `p_due` prioritizes earliest originating deadlines, and `p_tdur` prioritizes rows from slowest completed originating runs.
Result `p_reqf`/`p_until` select the exact persisted audit requested-delivery horizon, `p_sfrom`/`p_sto` select an inclusive persisted audit lifecycle start-date window, and `p_ffrom`/`p_fto` select an inclusive persisted audit lifecycle finish-date window, while `p_from`/`p_to` continue filtering snapshot-row requested dates; all audit windows are validated and exposed in all result export modes.
Result `p_deadf`/`p_deadt` select an inclusive effective deadline range for the originating persisted audit run, while `p_dead` remains the deadline-bearing switch; range bounds are validated and carried through result sink filtering and all export provenance. Result audit context now includes signed `audit_deadline_age_days` relative to the selected overdue as-of date or system date plus `deadline_age_reference_date` in every export mode. Result detail/summary schemas are `40`/`46`; `p_prev` filters persisted preview versus operational provenance through latest, exact-run, and sink reads; `p_safon`/`p_saf`/`p_safto` are propagated through the same paths; `p_dcnt` sorts by originating-run demand count, `p_dage` sorts by signed originating-run deadline age, `p_due` sorts by earliest originating-run deadline, and `p_tdur` sorts by originating-run audit duration after coverage and shortage-percentage precedence.
Watch typed JSON/NDJSON `filter_values` now also expose boolean `requested_overdue_only`, boolean `requested_deadline_only`, the selected as-of date, and inclusive audit finish-date bounds; the current watch JSON/NDJSON schema is `62`. History typed/metadata `filter_values` expose the same overdue boolean, deadline-only boolean, and as-of date plus the safety-stock range fields; current history detail and summary schemas are `26` and `43`, with persisted safety-stock context.
Direct overdue reads accept optional `iv_overdue_date` as an as-of boundary on the allocation-sink and audit APIs; when omitted, the current SAP system date is used.
Watch and history now expose optional `p_odate` overdue as-of and `p_dead` deadline-only selection-screen filters; watch additionally exposes inclusive persisted audit finish-date bounds `p_ffrom`/`p_fto` and the zero-safe `p_safon`/`p_saf`/`p_safto` safety-stock range; current watch schemas are CSV `59` and JSON/NDJSON `62`, while history detail/summary schemas are `26`/`43`.
Result and compare also accept `p_odate` when an overdue filter is enabled; result `p_latest` applies that same overdue predicate while choosing the newest run. Result detail/summary schemas are `40`/`46`, and comparison CSV/contextual JSON schemas are `98`.
Comparison JSON validation, authorization, and read failures use the same `schema_version: 98` envelope as successful contextual JSON output.
Purge now also accepts `p_deadf`/`p_deadt` for an inclusive effective requested-deadline range, `p_strat` for one persisted allocation strategy, `p_legacy` to select blank-strategy legacy runs, `p_from`/`p_to` for an inclusive audit start-date window, `p_ffrom`/`p_fto` for an inclusive persisted finish-date range, `p_tfrom`/`p_tto` for an inclusive completed audit-duration range in seconds, and `p_max` for a positive cap on finalized runs selected for purge; `p_strat` and `p_legacy` cannot be combined, one-sided horizons use `requested_on_from`, the audit start-date window remains below the exclusive cutoff, finish and duration filters exclude unfinished rows, runs with linked reservations are protected, reversed bounds, invalid strategies, and negative maximums are rejected, zero preserves unlimited retention behavior, and purge preview/execution schemas are CSV `25`/`26` and JSON `27`/`28` in both typed and untyped modes.
